## Final write OOM rollback regression — 2026-10-17

The Final transaction already converted allocation failures into a distinct
message that promises the prior successful snapshot is unchanged. Extended the
disposable IndexedDB failure-injection regression to throw `OutOfMemoryError`
while writing a new snapshot chunk, alongside `DataCloneError` and quota
failures. The assertions verify that active Final metadata, stored Final rows,
Inventory and History remain unchanged after each failure. This validates
rollback handling but does not replace a real-browser memory-pressure test.

## Historical comparison strong-identity conflict — 2026-10-17

Historical comparison backfill received a `matched` result with
`identityConflicts` when a unique high-priority ID matched one row but the same
MAC pointed to a second row. The code recorded the conflict yet continued to
copy blank fields from the selected row. It now blocks restoration for that
device and returns both candidates as an unresolved identity diagnostic. A
regression verifies that no model is copied from either row and that the
diagnostic contains both identities. Unit and identity tests pass; browser
comparison smoke remains open.

## Conflicting identity comparison counts — 2026-10-17

`compareFinals` retained strong-identifier conflicts as diagnostics but also
counted the selected row as modified and the competing row as absent. This
inflated dashboard KPI and detail totals. Full comparison now classifies the
pair as unresolved, marks both previous candidates as accounted for, and omits
the pair from added/modified/absent/unchanged counts. The streamed aggregate,
absence, modification, unchanged and event pages apply the same rule. Tests
cover exact KPI totals, status classification, candidate visibility and every
detail-page family; comparison, identity and analytics tests pass. Browser UI
smoke remains open.

## Weak-only comparison evidence — 2026-10-17

Weak hostname, Smartroom ID and IP evidence was already prevented from choosing
a match, but a weak-only row with no matching history fell through as an
addition. That made a status classifier say `unresolved` while the count said
`added`. The resolver now returns unresolved for candidates without any strong
identity, including when there are no weak hits; current Final comparisons and
all streamed detail pages suppress synthetic additions and absences. A new
strong identity with only a shared weak room token remains eligible as a new
device, so weak room membership cannot block a legitimate expansion. Focused
identity, enrichment, comparison, analytics and rendering tests pass. Browser
smoke remains open.

## Cursor-bounded selected Final deletion — 2026-10-17

Selective snapshot deletion previously materialized every snapshot metadata row
with `getAll()` before deleting selected Finals. It now scans the snapshot store
with a cursor inside the existing atomic transaction, deletes selected related
records as before, and retains only the newest surviving Final metadata needed
to restore the active pointer. A disposable IndexedDB regression covers 1,200
Final metadata rows, excludes a newer single-analysis snapshot from active
selection, and verifies that deleting the active Final selects the next newest
Final. It also confirms that a date-less Final keeps the prior empty-date sort
behavior and cannot outrank a dated snapshot. A source regression prevents
reintroducing `snapshots.getAll()`. Existing atomic relation and Inventory
preservation tests still pass; real-browser UI smoke remains open.

## Dashboard manufacturer and room KPI details — 2026-10-17

The manufacturer and room KPI cards now open complete aggregate lists with
100-row pagination. Selecting a value applies that manufacturer or room filter
to the Dashboard; imported labels are escaped and the rows come from the
selected status aggregate. Renderer tests cover pagination and escaping, and
source-wiring tests cover both KPI actions and their filter handlers. The
browser-only verification remains open, so parity requirement 499 stays
partial.

## Broken legacy autosave import — 2026-10-17

SQLite `app_autosaves` records remain stored under a namespaced legacy setting;
the browser Next runtime does not execute legacy application-state blobs. An
integration regression imports an autosave whose state references a missing
Final into a database that already has a valid active Final, then verifies the
active pointer and snapshot catalogue are unchanged. This closes the local
browser-import safety behavior only. The source v1.0.27 full ZIP and cleaned
database are absent from the repository, so their byte-for-byte preservation
cannot be verified; parity requirement 140 remains partial.

## Dashboard manufacturer/room KPI smoke coverage — 2026-10-17

The disposable browser-smoke harness now opens the manufacturer and room KPI
dialogs, checks that their full aggregate lists render, selects a value, and
confirms that the corresponding Dashboard filter is applied. Static wiring
tests and `node --check` cover the scenario source. The browser scenario itself
was not run because this turn is limited to code checks; Dashboard parity
requirement 499 remains partial pending an allowed runtime verification.

## Cursor-bounded startup Final recovery — 2026-10-17

Startup recovery previously called `listSnapshots()`, materializing every
snapshot metadata row before checking the active pointer and scanning backwards
for the newest complete Final. It now validates the active Final directly,
then walks the `by_created` index in reverse and checks each candidate's chunks
with a bounded cursor range, stopping at the first complete Final. Legacy
records with no `createdAt` are checked after dated candidates and remain first
in the catalogue as an undated/oldest fallback. Regressions prevent recovery
from using `listSnapshots()`, cover a broken newest Final and a date-less
fallback, and verify missing or
transient reads remain distinguishable. This reduces startup history memory;
browser restart smoke remains open.

## Grouped Dashboard change details — 2026-10-17

The selected-pair change report previously rendered one flat table row per
field event. The “modified” category now pages by changed device (100 groups
per page), keeping every changed field for one device together and preventing
the same device group from reappearing on the next page. Each group opens to
show model, address, room, severity, before/after values and available field
sources. Group pages stream the current Final, retain at most one page of
groups, and reconcile their event total against the exact comparison count.
Renderer grouping uses the shared strong identity order (internal ID, Device
ID, serial, primary MAC, alternate MAC), so a MAC change on the same persistent
device does not split its details into separate cards. Field severity is
derived per change, so a critical IP difference does not promote unrelated
manufacturer/model fields in the same device group.
Added and absent categories remain device-level entries. The selected-pair
“all” and “critical” tabs now combine those device entries with modified-device
groups, page by group count, and preserve exact event totals; the critical tab
keeps only critical fields inside modified groups. The period-mode report still
uses its separate event-index pagination. Tests cover 220 changed devices
across three IndexedDB pages, critical-only field filtering, exact event totals,
grouping, severity promotion and escaping. Browser UI validation is still open
under the current `file:` URL restriction.

## Dashboard chart scope against the Web reference — 2026-10-17

The Web `dashboard_service.py` replaces the movement series for dashboard
dynamics with the full upload-fleet timeline. Next derives that chart from all
Final metadata, independent of selected status; vendor ranking comes from the
selected-status dataset, field changes appear for `all`/`changed`, and missing
vendor rows for `all`/`missing`. A regression checks all four status selections
and the export model shared by PNG, HTML, and XLSX. Static parity is covered;
browser rendering remains open.

## PDF and analytics export source trace — 2026-10-17

The `ExportManager` PDF call sites in the PyQt source receive device lists
(`devices` or `current_devices`). `AnalyticsDialog.export_report()` separately
writes its generated report as TXT; it has no PDF action. The Web Dashboard
exports PNG/HTML, while Next also offers XLSX. Next maps PDF to the full
chunked Final export and keeps analytics TXT/XLSX/HTML and Dashboard
PNG/SVG/HTML/XLSX actions. This resolves the apparent analytics-PDF gap as a
source-trace ambiguity; export browser smoke remains open.

## Error banner dismissal — 2026-10-17

The reported error banner could remain visible after clicking its close button
because `.error-banner { display: flex }` overrode the browser's default hidden
display rule. Next now explicitly hides `.error-banner[hidden]`, and the close
handler clears the old message text as well as setting `hidden`. The shared UI
helper is tested for both show and dismiss state transitions; source checks
cover markup, CSS override, click-handler wiring, and portable-module inclusion.
Browser interaction verification remains open under the user-selected code-only
check scope.

## Results columns and Inventory XLSX — 2026-10-17

Compared PyQt `ColumnManager.ALL_COLUMNS` and `get_column_value()` with the
Next Inventory catalogue. Next was missing formatted MAC, OUI 3–6, note, added
date, and row number. Those fields are now selectable; OUI and MAC values are
normalized locally, and the date uses the Inventory first-observed timestamp.
The row number is one-based across visible pages, TSV chunk flushes, CSV, XLSX,
and PDF. Inventory XLSX now applies the selected column order and labels while
still streaming the full Inventory. Unit tests cover each derived field, legacy
settings, upgrade of older column preferences without losing their saved
choices, export order, and numbering. The manager now exposes the PyQt
"Удалить пользовательскую колонку" action; removing a column removes its
configuration but leaves values already stored on device records intact.
Browser UI verification remains open.

## PyQt settings and legacy result-column migration — 2026-10-17

Compared `SettingsManager`'s saved `visible_columns` and `custom_columns` with
the PyQt `ColumnManager.ALL_COLUMNS` and `get_column_value()` labels. The JSON
settings importer now restores all supported result-column labels, maps
`MAC (форматированный)` to the distinct formatted-MAC field, and recreates
custom column definitions. Custom columns remain hidden unless the source
settings list them as visible. Regression tests cover formatted MAC, OUI,
notes, added date, custom-column visibility, and the always-present row number.
Custom-column IDs are deterministic from the normalized label, so the settings
migrator and SQLite payload importer resolve legacy `custom_fields` to the
same key regardless of which file is imported first. A SQLite regression
checks that an imported field value is visible through its restored column.

## Custom source column mapping — 2026-10-17

Compared PyQt `ColumnMappingDialog` and `_process_chunk()` custom-field handling
with the Next import flows. The source review can now assign columns to
configured custom fields for Main, SmartRoom, and DDIO; batch mapping includes
the configured custom fields. Mapped values retain per-field source metadata.
Main and SmartRoom follow the normal blank-only merge priority. DDIO custom
values attach only through exact current MAC evidence, preserve known values,
and never create a device or replace a known device IP. Different DDIO custom
values remain bounded diagnostics. Tests cover mapping persistence, source
priority, and DDIO matching; browser UI verification remains open.

## Legacy SQLite import page handling — 2026-10-18

The installed SQLite-WASM helper consumes its 32-byte header separately and
then reads one full page at a time. A regular `Blob.stream()` can deliver a
larger chunk, and the helper drops the unread remainder; it can also repeat the
last short row batch through its iterator cleanup. Next now creates a bounded
page-aligned stream for the private IndexedDB copy and reads SQLite statements
directly, preserving each imported row once. The legacy `mac_history` schema in
the reference stores its observation time as `timestamp`, so that field is now
accepted alongside other timestamp aliases. A generated multi-page SQLite-WASM
database with 129 settings passes integrity and import tests, including exact
counts across the final partial 128-row batch. Four local sidecars
(`vendor_model_history.db`, `persistent_vendor_history.db`, `mac_history.db`,
and `mac_analyzer_stats.db`) pass WASM integrity/schema import with no skipped
tables; those copies contain no importable rows. Full import against the browser
IndexedDB VFS and UI remains open, so this does not close the parity requirement.

## Error dismissal and snapshot metadata memory — 2026-10-18

The error banner close action now has an accessible name and alert semantics.
Its bound click handler prevents default behavior, stops propagation, hides the
banner, and clears the previous message; a unit regression invokes that handler
instead of only checking that a listener was mentioned in source. Browser UI
interaction remains unverified because this task is restricted to code checks.

Database snapshot listing now walks the object store with an IndexedDB cursor.
The UI requests metadata without the potentially large diagnostics payload;
the default API still returns full metadata for callers that need it. A database
regression verifies the summary omits diagnostics while the compatible default
continues to return them.

## Custom fields in source mapping — 2026-10-18

PyQt lets the operator add a named custom field and select its source column in
the file mapping dialog. Next now exposes the same operation in each Main,
SmartRoom, and DDIO mapping card. The new field starts hidden in Inventory,
retains the field-level enrichment rules, and is saved with the exact normalized
source-header signature. Tests verify unique-column assignment, invalid column
rejection, duplicate mapping rejection, non-mutating configuration updates,
and preference restoration. A streamed three-file batch regression now checks
custom values from Main, SmartRoom, and exact-MAC DDIO through enrichment and
IndexedDB Inventory persistence, including field provenance. Browser
interaction remains open under the current code-only verification constraint.

The parity-registry validator now rejects progress prose that lacks an
explicit `Полностью`, `Выполнено`, `Частично`, or open status prefix. This
also corrected requirement 230, whose implementation evidence had not been
classified as partial while its browser smoke remained open.

Open backend-only requirements now point to their actual browser-only
counterparts, architecture documentation, and regression tests. The registry
validator requires implementation and test references for open rows too, so an
unsupported requirement stays visibly open without losing its traceability.

## Bounded enrichment history lookups — 2026-10-18

Historical switch-IP, Smartroom, and imported Smartroom lookups no longer build
one full list of keys from every enriched device before querying IndexedDB.
Enrichment now collects keys from windows of at most 500 devices and merges the
returned identity indexes without changing the existing blank-only fill rules.
Regressions verify exact 500/500/205 request windows for 1,205 unique switch
IPs, historical Smartroom IDs, and imported Smartroom IDs; they preserve each
mapping and retain the existing unique-ID and known-value behavior. This reduces
peak request-key memory; the full Final/Previous Final arrays and the final
evidence map remain separate open memory-parity work.

The enrichment summary now renders historical switch-IP evidence in pages of
100 rows with exact totals and candidate values. It iterates the existing Map
directly for the requested page instead of copying every candidate into a
second array and one large HTML table. Paging is covered by source-wiring tests;
the live browser interaction remains unverified.

## Relevant historical address pages — 2026-10-18

The cursor-paged evidence table now filters its indexed `by_switch` scan to IPs
with blank addresses in the current enrichment result or addresses filled from
historical evidence. This preserves the previous screen scope without retaining
the full historical evidence Map. Database tests cover exact filtered totals,
unrelated-IP exclusion, and 1,205-key pagination; source wiring verifies the
current-result filter and bounded lookup. Browser interaction remains open
under the selected code-only verification mode.

## IEEE reference refresh — 2026-10-18

Ran the repository's atomic IEEE updater against all five official CSV sources.
The embedded registry now contains 40,344 MA-L, 6,641 MA-M, 7,235 MA-S,
4,575 IAB, and 221 CID entries. The source diff adds one MA-S assignment,
`8C1F64860` (Gogo BA), without changing existing MA-S assignments; source SHA-256
values remain recorded in the asset metadata. A regression asserts that the new
36-bit prefix is available through the bundled lookup. Updater, parser, portable
lookup, and enrichment checks pass; backend lazy indexing and browser smoke stay
open.

## SQLite import skip visibility — 2026-10-18

Compared `LEGACY_SQLITE_TABLES` with the source SQLite schema in `server.py`.
The importer previously reported unsupported columns only for known tables and
silently omitted every table name it did not recognize. It now lists unknown
tables in `skippedTables` without querying their rows. The source's
`notification_settings`, `app_logs`, and `engineering_sessions` receive specific
reasons because they may contain notification secrets, sensitive log content,
or authentication sessions. A fake-runtime regression confirms these tables
and an arbitrary future table are reported while only the supported settings
table is read.

## Error banner repeat dismissal — 2026-10-18

The close button already hid the error banner, but every subsequent identical
`showError` call made it visible again. This could make a recurring asynchronous
error appear impossible to close. The banner now remembers a dismissed message:
repeated copies remain hidden, a different error can still be shown, and an
explicit clear after a successful action resets the suppression. Unit coverage
checks each transition. `pnpm check` passes; the browser click remains unverified
under the selected code-only verification mode.

## Bounded Inventory comparison pages — 2026-10-18

Single-file and scheduled partial analyses previously built one candidate map
for every current device before reconciliation. The Inventory query now yields
fixed pages to a synchronous consumer inside its read-only transaction, clears
each page after consumption, and avoids returning a second complete candidate
map. One incrementally populated identity index is reused for reconciliation
and comparison; duplicate Inventory IDs are retained once. Unit and IndexedDB
integration regressions cover page boundaries, candidate parity, alternate-MAC
identity, and record release. Browser smoke remains open under the selected
code-only verification mode.

## Multi-file comparison XLSX export — 2026-10-18

The PyQt multi-file dialog exports every directed pair to XLSX with an overall
file/device-count sheet, one detail sheet per pair, and one pair-statistics
sheet. Next previously offered only combined CSV. Next now streams equivalent
pair details and statistics to XLSX, preserves ambiguous-identity diagnostics,
reuses one pair result for its detail and statistics sheets, and retains the
existing all-pairs CSV option. File System Access writes the workbook as a
stream when available. Unit tests validate the generated workbook and row
counts; the real browser picker interaction remains open under the selected
code-only verification mode.

## Analysis and comparison cancellation — 2026-10-18

The global progress cancel button only aborted work through `state.controller`;
single-file analysis and two-file comparison stored dedicated controllers,
so the visible button could not stop those operations. The cancel action now
aborts the global, single-file, and two-file controllers. Single-file analysis
passes its signal to file parsing and enrichment; two-file comparison passes it
to each row stream, treats cancellation separately from errors, releases its
controller, and restores the run button. Multi-file comparison and both exports
use the same cancel action; XLSX cancellation aborts the pending output write.
Tests cover importer aborts, multi-file stream cancellation, and UI wiring.
Browser click verification remains open under the selected code-only mode.

## Local and external notification parity boundary — 2026-10-21

The PyQt reference's `NotificationService.on_analysis_complete` sends the
source filename, device count, address count, room count, and processing time
through email, Telegram, or Slack. Its settings dialog also stores an SMTP
password, Telegram bot token, and Slack webhook in a local JSON file. The
browser Next build has no trusted delivery backend, and the engineering guide
prohibits uploading equipment or network data. The applicable Web standalone behavior is implemented as local event preferences
and a bounded IndexedDB journal for analysis, quality, and backup events. It has
no external channels or delivery calls. Requirement 402 remains partial because
the PyQt external sends are not implemented; requirement 673 stays open until a
privacy-preserving delivery architecture exists.

## Weak IP identity normalization — 2026-10-18

The identity resolver previously tokenized IP with generic text normalization.
The shared normalizer also accepted malformed IPv6 group counts and extracted
an IPv4 substring from arbitrary surrounding text. It now validates IPv4 and
IPv6 forms, canonicalizes IPv6 compression and embedded IPv4, and rejects
malformed values before creating identity evidence. Tests cover IPv4 leading
zeroes, equivalent compressed/expanded IPv6, IPv4-mapped IPv6, invalid group
counts, embedded text, and MAC-shaped values. IP remains weak diagnostic
evidence and never causes an automatic identity match.

## Original-file metadata cursor — 2026-10-18

`Database.listOriginalFiles` used `getAll(range)` to return metadata for a
snapshot, materializing each source-file Blob reference before discarding it.
The method now walks the snapshot's compound-key range with an IndexedDB cursor
and returns only metadata. Its integration regression makes `getAll` on the
`originalFiles` store throw and verifies the returned rows omit Blob content.

## Partial Inventory comparison memory — 2026-10-18

Single-file and scheduled analyses previously built an Inventory candidate
index to reconcile device identities, then independently deduplicated those
same records and built a second identity index for comparison. The two steps
now share one Inventory projection and one identity index. The candidate Map is
cleared before comparison begins, and `compareFinals` accepts the already-built
index only when it references the exact same baseline array. A regression
checks canonical alternate-MAC recovery, exact changed fields, no synthetic
absences, and release of the candidate map.

## Previous Final identity index reuse — 2026-10-18

Enrichment already built a strong-identity index for carrying blank fields from
the previous Final, but the persistence comparison rebuilt the same index over
the identical compact device array. The coordinator now constructs the index
once, shares it with enrichment, and passes that exact reference to the streamed
comparison. Enrichment validates that the index points to the supplied baseline
array and falls back to building its own when it does not. A regression wraps
the index token lookup and confirms the provided index performs Previous Final
matching; coordinator wiring is checked in the build tests. The compact baseline
and identity index remain resident because both are required for enrichment
and exact comparison.

## Data-quality IPv6 validation — 2026-10-18

The Next data-quality validator accepted an IPv6 value with a single unmatched
leading or trailing colon because it discarded empty groups before checking the
group count. It now rejects those malformed forms while preserving valid
compressed and fully expanded addresses. A regression checks both device IP
and switch IP issue counts. This aligns the browser validator with the PyQt
reference's `ipaddress.ip_address` validation; user-interface verification stays
open under code-only checks.

## Exact current-room mapping evidence — 2026-10-18

Current Smartroom ID mapping used the five-entry MAC sample length as its
`deviceCount`, so room provenance understated support whenever more than five
devices agreed. The accumulator now counts every matching current device while
keeping the MAC evidence sample capped at five. A regression uses seven
matching devices and confirms the exact count independently of the five-MAC
sample. Browser verification remains open under code-only checks.

## Literal data-quality source names — 2026-10-18

Quality source aggregation previously passed the `source` label through the
empty-marker normalizer used for device attributes. That changed a literal
`Unknown` source label to lowercase `unknown`, unlike PyQt's exact trimmed text
count. Source categories now preserve their original non-empty label, and a
regression verifies the dominant-source name and count.

## Selectable rows in device context tables — 2026-10-18

Context actions existed for device rows outside Inventory, but those rows did
not remain visibly selected when clicked and had no keyboard selection path.
They now select per table on click, Enter, or Space; opening their context menu
with right-click or Shift+F10 also selects the row. The selected state updates
`aria-selected` and uses a theme-aware outline/background. Unit tests cover
single-row selection and independent tables; source wiring tests cover the
mouse and keyboard paths. Browser verification remains open.

## Escape cancellation for every active analysis — 2026-10-18

The Esc shortcut previously aborted only the shared enrichment controller,
while the cancel button also targets dedicated single-file and two-file
comparison controllers. Esc now calls the same cancellation path as the button.
The shared helper de-duplicates controllers and aborts each active one once; a
unit test covers all three controller slots and duplicate references, while a
build-wiring assertion verifies the shortcut uses that helper. Browser
interaction remains open.

The global shortcut handler also suppressed the browser's native Escape action
for every open dialog, while only explicitly closing the device-details dialog.
Escape now closes a context menu first, leaves an open native dialog to its
browser close behavior, dismisses a visible error banner, then cancels active
work. It only prevents the key's default action when the app handled Escape.
Focused tests cover the priority and the wiring, including the error message
dismissal path. Browser interaction remains open under the selected code-only
verification constraint.

## Release current Final before extended Dashboard aggregation — 2026-10-18

The selected Dashboard previously retained the complete compact current-Final
array as a preloaded payload while calculating delayed rankings and topology.
For the usual “all devices” status, the detail stage now rereads the complete
Final through its existing bounded IndexedDB chunk iterator, allowing the
current projection to be collected after KPI calculation. The “missing” status
now passes a one-byte-per-row absent-position mask and rereads matching
baseline rows from bounded chunks instead of retaining a second absent-device
object list. If a user returns to Dashboard before those details finish, the
selected pair is recomputed so the new mask matches the reloaded baseline. Unit
tests cover comparison positions and chunk offsets; build wiring covers both
the chunk reread and missing-status re-entry. The full `pnpm check` passed all
850 tests. The pairwise identity/comparison projections still coexist during
exact comparison, so the memory requirement remains open.

The comparison previously retained its baseline identity index through a
status-classifier closure even when the selected Dashboard status was “all” or
“missing”. The app now requests that classifier only for “changed” and
“unchanged”, where it is required to filter full streamed detail rows. A unit
regression verifies the classifier is omitted when not requested and still
exists by default; the app wiring selects it from the status filter. This lets
the baseline projection be collected after pair calculation for “all” and
“missing”, while preserving exact classification in the other modes.

## Escape recovery and portable artifact inspection — 2026-10-18

The built standalone HTML contains the error dismissal button, the Escape
dispatcher, and native-dialog Escape behavior; it has one inline application
script and no external script dependencies. `check-portable-artifact.mjs`
compiled the app and analytics worker. The release ZIP contains 211 entries;
required source, documentation, IEEE reference data, licenses, and the
autonomous HTML are present. The embedded HTML hash matches the built file
(`FBB81ACF17550A9F72CDFE1EA7711B3BB434CEDF1FAD8B54ABC527C809D91F9F`), and
the ZIP's SHA-256 was verified against its sidecar.
The parity registry validates 187 unique requirements. Release readiness still
blocks publication because 151 requirements remain open; no GitHub Release is
published. Browser interaction remains unverified under the selected code-only
constraint.

## Current-status Dashboard mask and error-banner rebuild — 2026-10-18

The Dashboard's “changed” and “unchanged” detail filters no longer retain a
baseline identity-index closure. Comparison computes one byte per current row
after strong-identity matching; additions, conflicts, ambiguous rows, and rows
outside the active filters remain zero. The detail stage applies that mask to
the bounded Final chunk stream and re-entry rebuilds any non-default status
mask from the selected pair. Focused comparison, analytics, build-wiring,
error-banner, and shortcut checks passed (184 tests); the subsequent full
`pnpm check` passed all 850 tests.

The source contains the close button, Escape path, and repeated-error
suppression from the earlier regression fix. The autonomous HTML was rebuilt
and syntax-checked after this change, so the generated `dist/` copy includes
those fixes. The actual browser click remains unverified because verification
is limited to code checks. Requirement 46 remains partial while pairwise comparison holds
both compact projections at once and browser smoke is open; requirement 677
remains partial until the browser click can be observed.

## Verify separate v1.0.27 source package — 2026-10-18

The earlier audit incorrectly said the v1.0.27 complete archive was absent.
Read-only inspection found it beside the Next repository at
`Mac_analyzer_Pro/portable/complete/MAC-Analyzer-Pro-v1.0.27-Complete.zip`.
Its ZIP directory contains 494 entries, including SQLite databases; only entry
names and metadata were inspected, not database contents. Its current SHA-256
is `AE5A4860F37B4215933A8B0C8B2F0DF258AEC5B171557E6AA27E0A3A2A02A672`.
The archive stays outside the Next GitHub repository because it contains
user-data databases. No original checksum was available for a byte-for-byte
comparison against the first delivery, so #140 remains partial.

## IndexedDB v11 preservation regression — 2026-10-18

The #52 registry entry had conflated the browser database migration with
read-only migration of external PyQt SQLite files. A disposable IndexedDB v11
fixture now upgrades to the current v26 schema and verifies that Final metadata
and chunks, Inventory including a custom value, History, an original-file Blob,
the active Final pointer, and user settings all survive. The database migration
test file passes 140/140 tests; the full `pnpm check` passes 851/851 tests,
including lint, parity registry validation, autonomous HTML/worker compilation,
and full ZIP verification (211 entries). The release-readiness gate now reports
150/187 open requirements; no release is published.

## Reduce Dashboard room-aggregation working set — 2026-10-18

`buildSelectedAnalytics` and switch-anomaly analysis previously materialized
room descriptor objects for every current-Final device while both selected
compact projections were resident. The room-location evidence is now indexed
once, and each descriptor is computed and consumed during a direct row pass.
This removes one full per-device object array from the comparison peak while
preserving inferred room IDs, unassigned vendor/model counts, and strict
majority anomaly totals. Targeted analytics/build-wiring checks pass (144
tests); the full `pnpm check` passes all 852 tests, including lint, parity
registry validation, autonomous HTML/worker compilation, and ZIP verification.
The two compact Final projections still coexist during exact pairwise
comparison, so #46 remains partial.

## Stream selected Dashboard comparison from Final chunks — 2026-10-18

The selected Dashboard no longer loads current Final into a full compact array.
It keeps one compact baseline and reads current Final in three sequential chunk
passes: strong-identity comparison/status masks, room and filter evidence, then
KPI/ranking/room coverage aggregates. Each pass checks the stored Final
signature, so a concurrent revision aborts the mixed calculation. The page
shows the current pass and row progress. Room coverage retains only compact
identity-to-change flags for exact duplicate-identity handling, not device
records; added/absent/modified/unchanged rows are fetched from IndexedDB only
when their detail pages are opened.

Regression coverage compares the streamed result against the full pair for
status masks, exact KPIs, field and vendor summaries, room moves, switch-IP
strict-majority anomalies, weak ambiguity, strong-identity conflicts, and
duplicate identities. A revision-change test confirms the calculation stops
before a mixed pass. `tests/analytics.test.js`, `tests/comparison.test.js`, and
`tests/build-config.test.js` pass (181 focused tests at the time of this entry).
The later full check passes 858 tests and verifies the autonomous HTML and
211-entry ZIP. Browser smoke remains open under the user's code-only
verification choice; requirement 46 therefore remains partial and no release
is published.

## Stop browser-owned work when the app closes — 2026-10-18

Closing a tab now aborts the active import/analysis/comparison controllers,
invalidates pending Dashboard work, clears autosave/analytics/engineering
intervals, and stops both named batch schedules and restored legacy task
schedulers. A page entering the back-forward cache is left intact. Cleanup is
implemented in the portable `src/core/page-lifecycle.js` helper and is
behavior-tested for both normal page closure and BFCache in
`tests/page-lifecycle.test.js`; app wiring is covered by
`tests/build-config.test.js`. The focused suite passes. This implements the
browser lifecycle equivalent for orderly shutdown, while OS
SIGINT/SIGTERM remain unavailable to a browser tab, so registry requirement
390 remains partial. The full `pnpm check` passes 861 tests and verifies the
78-module autonomous HTML plus the 213-entry ZIP. Browser smoke stays open
under the user's code-only verification choice.

## Recover a Final referenced by a legacy autosave — 2026-10-18

SQLite import first reads only the compact snapshot references from autosave
rows, then retains mappings only for those IDs as it streams snapshots. It maps
references to the namespaced IDs imported from that same database.
The Data view lists imported autosave slots and lets the user explicitly
activate a linked Final after confirmation. It preserves the current and
historical snapshots; missing or non-Final IDs are rejected, and the original
autosave state remains stored. Files and unfinished rows from the old workspace
are not restored because their browser file handles are not portable. Tests
cover source-ID mapping, broken-reference safety, bounded cursor reads, and
activation; browser restore smoke remains open, so requirement 140 stays
partial.

## Restore compatible preferences from a legacy autosave — 2026-10-18

Imported autosaves now expose a separate, explicitly confirmed settings
recovery action. It reads only the selected autosave record and rejects state
larger than 1 MiB before JSON parsing. The normalizer whitelists current local
preferences (theme, merge strategy, vendor detector, merge fields, OUI formats,
Inventory column order, visibility and widths, source-column mappings, dashboard widgets/filters, and IP
mappings. Reference file-header objects are converted to stable strings, and
legacy custom result-column keys become deterministic Next custom fields with
their source-column mapping restored for the selected file); it does not restore tokens,
external API credentials, paths, source files, or unfinished rows. Existing
Finals and analysis results are unaffected. Unit, IndexedDB, and UI-wiring tests
cover the byte bound, supported settings, secret/path exclusion, and persistence.
Browser restore smoke remains open under the user's code-only verification
choice, so parity requirement 140 stays partial.

## Dashboard filter parity follow-up — 2026-10-18

The earlier entries in this audit describe the implementation at the time they
were written. Dashboard filters now have code and test coverage for full Final
data, status masks, exact manufacturer/room options, KPI and ranked summaries,
and period/history details. The ordinary and chunked analytics paths now use the
same manufacturer fallback: a legacy `vendor` value is used when `manufacturer`
is empty or a normalized empty marker. Dashboard search checks scalar fields
and array contents, including alternate MACs, in one pass without constructing
the `Object.values().filter().map()` temporary arrays; nested object contents
remain excluded while array values remain searchable.
Regressions cover legacy vendor aliases across regular and streamed analytics,
manufacturer filters/options, search across field boundaries, alternate MACs,
and nested-object exclusion. `pnpm check` passed 940 tests and verified the
autonomous HTML and 217-entry ZIP. Browser UI smoke remains open under the
user's code-only verification choice, so requirements 120 and 499 remain
partial and the release gate remains closed.

## Сверка покрытия требований с текущим Web-эталоном — 2026-10-18

Сопоставил текущий `../Mac_analyzer_Pro/PARITY_STATUS.json` с закреплёнными в
Next `tests/fixtures/pyqt-parity-source-ids.json` и
`tests/fixtures/pyqt-parity-source-text.json`: все 178 исходных ID совпали,
пропусков и лишних ID нет; все 178 текстов требований также совпали точно.
Реестр Next содержит 187 строк: 178 исходных требований и 9 дополнительных
проверок самого приложения. Тест `tests/parity-registry.test.js` независимо
проверяет наличие всех исходных ID в Next-реестре.

Эта сверка доказывает полноту и актуальность списка требований относительно
текущего Web-эталона, но не подтверждает поведение каждого экрана или действия.
Текущий `pnpm check:release-readiness` всё ещё оставляет 150 из 187 требований
открытыми; среди причин — непроверенный browser smoke, ограничения автономной
архитектуры и функции, требующие разрешённой внешней передачи данных. Релиз не
публикуется, пока обязательные требования и проверки не закрыты.

## Ограничение памяти batch enrichment — 2026-10-19

Массовое обогащение теперь применяет historical и импортированные Smartroom-маппинги по запросам ограниченных lookup-пакетов IndexedDB: агрегирующая полная Map больше не удерживается между пакетами; каждый пакет ограничен 500 уникальными ключами. Ключ для чтения страницы нормализуется через `keyText`, поэтому значения маппингов остаются точными при различии регистра исходного Smartroom ID. Обычный одиночный запуск сохраняет полный индекс для постраничного отчёта. Регрессии сверяют заполненные помещения и точные счётчики для обоих источников, app wiring и потоковый режим; полный release gate и browser smoke остаются открытыми.


## Потоковый fallback топологии — 2026-10-19

HTML-экспорт полной топологии уже читал Final ограниченными чанками, но fallback без File System Access накапливал все строковые фрагменты до создания Blob. Теперь он кодирует выдачу через TransformStream, ждёт backpressure и формирует Blob из readable-потока; abort/error закрывает writer и обрабатывает отклонение reader. Регрессия сверяет байтовый UTF-8 документ на 305 строках, включая кириллицу и escaping. Browser smoke остаётся открытым по выбранному ограничению кодовых проверок.


## Потоковый fallback экспорта активного Final — 2026-10-21

CSV/TXT/JSON/YAML/HTML-писатели активного Final уже выдавали ограниченные чанки, однако без File System Access приложение выбирало отдельные Blob-функции, накапливавшие строки до создания скачиваемого файла. Теперь в браузерах с TransformStream writer подключён к Response и соблюдает backpressure; если TransformStream отсутствует, однопозиционный ReadableStream sink кодирует текст непосредственно в скачиваемый Blob, не накапливая массив строк. Только когда недоступны и ReadableStream/Response, остаётся прежний Blob fallback. Для YAML добавлен отсутствовавший MIME type; суффиксы YAML и HTML исправлены с некорректных `.yaml;charset=utf-8` и `.html;charset=utf-8` на `.yaml` и `.html`. Регрессии сверяют точные байты всех пяти форматов на 405 устройствах, включая кириллицу и escaping, и проверяют поток на 2 000 частей, abort и wiring ветки скачивания. Release gate и browser smoke остаются открытыми.

Дополнительно закреплена стабильность Windows host smoke: shutdown token передаётся парсеру как `--shutdown-token=<значение>`, чтобы случайное значение с начальным дефисом не трактовалось как отдельный CLI-флаг. Повторный runtime smoke прошёл.

## Освобождение Final ID-проверки — 2026-10-21

После прохода обогащения Final проверял уникальность внутреннего ID через `Set`, сохранявший ещё одну ссылку на каждый результат. После успешной проверки set немедленно очищается до передачи результата следующим этапам; конфликт всё ещё прерывает Final с той же диагностикой. Build-wiring регрессия требует очистку после ID-check, а `tests/enrichment.test.js` подтверждает отказ на дублирующихся ID.


## Потоковый JSON fallback для файловых отчётов — 2026-10-19

Общий xportJsonFile уже использовал построчный writePrettyJson, но без File System Access складывал все его чанки в массив до создания Blob. Теперь при наличии TransformStream JSON-кодировщик пишет напрямую в UTF-8 writable с backpressure; скачивание строит конечный Blob из readable-потока. Старый parts fallback оставлен для браузеров без TransformStream. Регрессия сверяет байты и JSON roundtrip на 1 205 устройствах, wiring тест проверяет приложение. Ограничения исходных массивов и browser smoke остаются открытыми.


## Latin-1 fallback импорта больших файлов — 2026-10-19

Когда большой импортированный файл содержит невалидный UTF-8 и Windows-1251 (включая байт 0x98), fallback Latin-1 формировал массив 16-КиБ строковых частей, а затем второй полный текст через join. Теперь декодер напрямую конкатенирует блоки в результирующую строку, оставляя только неизбежный итоговый текст. Regression проверяет 70 001 байт, включая два полных блока и хвост, и сохраняет U+0098. Импортёр всё ещё строит полный разобранный результат там, где его требует текущий parser; этот более широкий memory gap остаётся открытым.

## Потоковое восстановление файла legacy SQLite queue — 2026-10-19

`readLegacyTaskQueueFileFromSQLite()` читает Base64-поле порциями по 64 КиБ. Ранее декодированные Uint8Array складывались в `parts[]`, поэтому сверх итогового Blob удерживался второй массив всего файла. Теперь браузер с TransformStream передаёт блоки через `WritableStream` в `Response.blob()` с backpressure; при отсутствии этих API остаётся совместимый Blob fallback. Ошибка чтения отменяет writer, а SQLite/VFS закрываются прежним `finally`. Регрессия проверяет байты, MIME type, прогресс, оценку ёмкости и закрытие базы на файле больше одного блока. Это закрывает промежуточную копию для этого пути, но сам итоговый Blob остаётся целым файлом.

Проверка панели ошибки: кнопка закрывает баннер, очищает текст и восстанавливает его исходное положение даже когда сообщение было перенесено в открытый `<dialog>`. Повтор одного и того же фонового сообщения после закрытия подавляется, чтобы баннер не открывался немедленно заново. Unit-тесты проверяют закрытие и поведение при повторе; browser smoke пользователь ранее ограничил кодовыми проверками, поэтому видимое поведение в открытом автономном HTML остаётся непроверенным.

## Столбец появлений в диалоге префиксов MAC5 — 2026-10-19

PyQt показывает для каждого префикса MAC5 число строк `mac_history` с этим
префиксом и экспортирует таблицу в XLSX. Next ранее не показывал число
появлений и не давал отдельный экспорт из диалога. Теперь счётчик строится
последовательным обходом чанков обычных Final и страниц импортированной
PyQt `mac_history`; импортированные из SQLite Finals пропускаются, чтобы не
дублировать ту же историю. В памяти остаются только счётчики для префиксов
результата, а метрика выбранного Final остаётся отдельной. Таблица показывает исходный/форматированный префикс, модель,
источник и число наблюдений; XLSX содержит те же значения. Код покрыт тестами
коллектора, HTML renderer и app wiring. Строка 534 остаётся частичной до
browser smoke.

## Сохранение текущих SQLite movement timestamp/source — 2026-10-19

PyQt `mac_movements` записывает `timestamp` и `source_file`, но импортёр Next
ранее читал только `changed_at` и `source`. Поэтому current-schema движения
могли получать время импорта и терять имя файла. Маппер теперь предпочитает
поля текущей схемы, сохраняя поддержку старых aliases; regression проверяет
нормализованное время, исходный файл и нормализацию `switch_port`. Проверка
находится в `tests/sqlite-importer.test.js`. Сверка выявила такой же разрыв в
`mac_history`: его `timestamp` и `source_file` также теперь сохраняются как
`observedAt`/`source`, с регрессией фактической PyQt-схемы.

Сверка этих же sidecar-баз обнаружила, что `oui_vendor_history` и
`mac5_model_history` импортировались только как lookup-правила и теряли
`occurrences`, `first_seen` и `last_seen`. Импорт теперь сохраняет все три
значения в записях правил, а `VendorModelHistoryAccumulator.addMappingRule()`
использует точное количество и диапазон исходных дат при построении отчёта.
Schema-shaped SQLite→IndexedDB, portable archive round-trip и агрегаторный
тесты проверяют значения для обоих типов правил.

## Максимальное число пар в массовом плане — 2026-10-19

Планировщик массового объединения сначала сохраняет прежний порядок предпочтения по минимальному расстоянию дат. Если из-за выбора одного Main допустимый файл оставался без пары, поиск augmenting path повторно назначает занятый источник на альтернативный файл и сохраняет one-to-one правило, пока существует допустимое назначение. Отсортированные кандидаты объединяются min-heap по одному head-элементу каждой Main-строки, без второго полного массива рёбер. Регрессия покрывает случай «две пары возможны, жадный первый выбор оставляет одну». Другие пары не меняются в прежнем nearest-first сценарии. Строка 658 остаётся частичной до UI E2E автономного HTML.

## Статистика индивидуальной истории MAC — 2026-10-19

PyQt показывает для MAC число появлений, файлов, изменений параметров, уникальных производителей и моделей. Next уже показывал число появлений и файлов, но не воспроизводил три остальных счётчика. Добавлены KPI и поля в TXT/HTML отчетах; уникальные vendor/model считаются по всем appearance Final и пропускают пустые значения и `Unknown`. Миграция v26→v27 добавляет безопасный compound index `(deviceId, type)` без изменения событий; `historyPage()` получает точное число `modified` через `count()` и не сканирует полный журнал в память. Регрессии проверяют сохранение прежних записей и точные числа при одновременных modified/added событиях. Строка 476 остаётся частичной до browser smoke.
# Продолжение проверки паритета

Индивидуальная аналитика устройства больше не копирует уже упорядоченные по
времени массивы появлений и событий истории ради повторной сортировки. Порядок
сначала проверяется линейным проходом; резервные несортированные входы копируются
и сортируются без изменения данных вызывающей стороны. Подсчёт событий `modified`
также идёт прямым проходом вместо временного массива `filter`. Регрессия проверяет
переиспользование штатных страниц IndexedDB и корректную сортировку резервных
входов. Общий пункт 25 остаётся частичным: ограничения полного сравнения и
актуальная browser smoke проверка всё ещё открыты.

HTML-экспорт индивидуальной аналитики теперь собирает таблицу появлений
партиями по 100 строк. Ячейки продолжают HTML-экранироваться, а итоговый документ
совпадает с прежним заголовком и footer. Тест проверяет 1 001 строку через 11
порций, форматирование документа и защиту от вставленного HTML; wiring-регрессия
проверяет, что приложение использует пакетные helper-функции.

Открытие индивидуальной аналитики раньше для каждой строки каждого Final строило
новый одноэлементный identity index и выполняло ranking. Теперь strong token set
выбранного устройства строится один раз, а строки сверяются по тем же MAC/alternate
MAC и strong-field правилам без временных candidate maps. Differential unit test
сверяет оптимизированный matcher с shared resolver на разных сильных полях и
weak-only случаях; source-wiring тест проверяет положение matcher вне циклов.

Постраничная подгрузка журнала раньше копировала все уже загруженные события
через spread и заново рендерила полную таблицу на каждом нажатии. Теперь DOM
получает только строки новой страницы; payload хранит последнюю страницу, а
отдельный cumulative count показывает число всех уже отображённых событий.
Полный HTML/XLSX экспорт по-прежнему читает историю курсорами из IndexedDB.
Регрессии проверяют точное число строк, ограниченный payload и отсутствие
повторного spread/render.

Повторный клик по кнопке во время чтения следующего IndexedDB-курсора теперь
игнорируется, кнопка отключается и показывает состояние загрузки; в `finally`
состояние восстанавливается после успеха или ошибки. Это предотвращает повторное
добавление одной и той же страницы при медленном чтении.

Dashboard fallback, который восстанавливает точные счётчики перед удалением
списков изменений, теперь считает все три totals за один проход. Временные массивы
`filter()` на событиях каждого изменённого устройства удалены; подсчёт изменённых
и полностью изменённых комнат также объединён в один проход. Регрессия закрепляет
текущую семантику field aliases и exact critical-event counters.

## Error banner close target — 2026-10-19

Длинный непрерывный текст ошибки мог переполнить flex-строку и вытеснить крестик
за видимую область; маленькая кнопка также усложняла закрытие. Текст теперь может
сжиматься и переноситься по любым границам, кнопка имеет фиксированную область
44×44 px. `tests/error-banner.test.js` проверяет эти CSS-инварианты и сохраняет
проверки click, repeat suppression, Escape и переноса баннера в `<dialog>`.
Кодовый тест проходит; визуальный runtime click остаётся непроверенным из-за
выбранного ограничения browser smoke.

## Потоковая первичная загрузка встроенного OUI — 2026-10-19

Инициализация встроенного справочника ранее вызывала `readTable()`, который
одновременно создавал полный текст CSV, массив строк и полный массив OUI-записей.
Теперь CSV читается пакетами по 500 строк, и каждая нормализованная партия сразу
сохраняется в IndexedDB. При fetch fallback используется Blob, без промежуточной
полной текстовой строки. Source-wiring regression подтверждает batch size,
постраничную запись и отсутствие eager reader. Встроенная portable CSV-строка
всё ещё загружается как часть автономного HTML; её размер не устранён этим
изменением.

После создания `File` строка `__MAC_ANALYZER_OUI_CSV__` освобождается из
локального binding и `globalThis`; до инициализации она остаётся embedded для
автономного режима. Build-config regression проверяет порядок создания файла и
очистки обеих ссылок.

## Единый источник встроенного OUI — 2026-10-19

Проверка portable build обнаружила, что HTML включал одновременно устаревший
`assets/oui.csv` (3 791 821 байт) и актуальный IEEE JSON. В CSV было 39 749
уникальных MA-L-префиксов, тогда как официальный JSON содержит 40 344 MA-L
назначения; CSV не содержал 595 актуальных назначений. Начальное заполнение
IndexedDB теперь берёт MA-L непосредственно из JSON пакетами до 500 записей,
а автономная HTML-сборка перестала встраивать дублирующий CSV. Файл остаётся
доступным в исходном проекте/полном ZIP. Версия seed берётся из `generatedAt`.
Обновление перезаписывает только записи с provenance встроенного источника;
миграция прежней версии `2026.10` также обновляет записи стандартного seed
источника, только если глобальная настройка подтверждает, что последним
источником был встроенный каталог. Пользовательский импорт и правила,
обученные из истории, сохраняются. Регрессии проверяют полный MA-L count,
ограниченный размер пакета, обновление старых seed записей и защиту импортных
и обученных правил. Если прежняя база содержит импортированные правила без
per-record source и последним источником помечен пользовательский файл, старый
seed нельзя отличить от этих правил; такой смешанный legacy случай остаётся
открытым. Browser runtime не проверялся.

Перед этой миграцией добавлена fail-closed проверка payload: version/UTC timestamp,
все ожидаемые строки MA-L/MA-M/MA-S/IAB, валидность vendor indexes, полный CID
catalog, суммарное количество и пять source metadata/count/SHA-256 записей.
Проверка выполняется до кеширования registry, OUI batch writes и сборки lookup
index. Регрессия искажает MA-L count, vendor index, CID count и source hash и
подтверждает отказ вместо принятия частичного справочника.

## IndexedDB full-store read guard — 2026-10-19

Read-only audit `rg '\.getAll\(' src/data src/core src/app.js` нашёл три runtime
вызова, все на точных индексных ключах с `count=2` для разрешения двусмысленных
Inventory-кандидатов. Полных `getAll()` чтений Final, Inventory, History или
архивов нет. Добавлен source regression, который обходит все JS-модули `src/`
и требует, чтобы каждый IndexedDB `getAll` оставался exact-key ограниченным
двумя кандидатами. Это не меняет runtime; тест предотвращает возврат к полной
материализации больших stores.

## Multi-file selector escaping — 2026-10-19

Селектор направленных пар multi-file сравнения теперь экранирует значение
`pair.id` в `option.value` вместе с обоими выбранными именами файлов. Идентификатор
сейчас генерируется из числовых индексов, но explicit escaping защищает DOM sink,
если его формат изменится. `tests/build-config.test.js` закрепляет все три
интерполяции в этом шаблоне.

## Spreadsheet formula protection in CSV/TSV — 2026-10-19

All CSV and TSV cell writers now share `src/core/csv-cell.js`. Quoting still
escapes delimiters and quotes; text cells with optional leading whitespace or
control characters followed by `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` receive a leading apostrophe
so spreadsheet software treats imported content as text. Actual numeric values
remain unprefixed. This covers Final, Inventory/Search, cluster analytics,
file and multi-file comparisons, IP mappings, temporal/vendor history, and
single-file analysis exports, including streamed writers. `tests/csv-cell.test.js`
checks the shared encoders, representative CSV/TSV exports, and statically guards
all runtime writers against bypassing the shared encoder; existing serializer
tests continue to check normal output compatibility. Browser spreadsheet-open
behavior remains unverified under the selected code-only check mode.

## Avoid nested MAC-alias copies in comparison projections — 2026-10-19

`compactComparisonDevice()` now reuses the detached/read-only `alternateMacs`
array instead of allocating another array per compact Final row. Other
object-valued fields keep their shallow-copy isolation. Identity and comparison
helpers normalize aliases into temporary values and do not mutate the shared
array. `tests/compact-final-analytics.test.js` verifies exact alternate-MAC
change detection, source-array immutability, and isolation of other objects.
This reduces duplicate nested data but does not remove the in-memory compact
baseline and identity index; parity item #61 remains open.

## Indexed identity lookups for first-baseline Dashboard pages — 2026-10-19

FinalAnalytics schema v5 stores each compact baseline device chunk with multiEntry keys for its normalized identity tokens. The IndexedDB lookup returns at most three rows per queried token, enough to preserve the identity resolver’s two candidate positions and overflow signal. Current Dashboard chunks are matched against a sparse index whose positions remain the original baseline offsets, so no full baseline device array is required for added, unchanged, modified-device, modified-event, or modified-group pages when the selected baseline is the first snapshot and needs no historical backfill. The v27→v28 migration adds the derived index without replacing user stores or records, and the streaming builder commits metadata only after all chunks have been written. For the first baseline, identity diagnostic pages now use the sparse identity index and preserve the exact ambiguous/conflict counts, row order, details, and pagination verified against the full resolver. Later baselines now build a signature-bound FinalAnalytics backfill index one chunk at a time, resolving historical rows through their sparse identity indexes. Comparison detail pages stream that derived index, and deleting Final history invalidates derived backfill caches. At that point the initial selected-pair summary still loaded its compact baseline; the following audit section records the streaming implementation that removes this limitation. Absent pages reuse bounded identity lookup, a Uint8Array matched-position mask, and a chunked baseline pass; tests cover exact totals, filtered roster, pagination, and weak-token ambiguity. Parity item #61 remains partial.

## Stream selected-pair comparison baseline — 2026-10-19

The initial Dashboard summary no longer loads a full compact baseline or builds a full in-memory identity index. It reads the signed FinalAnalytics baseline in chunks and resolves each current chunk against a sparse strong-identity index queried from IndexedDB. The same stream counts exact statuses, filters room evidence around ambiguous identities, and reuses the indexed historical backfill cache while retaining only the exact-sized position masks and aggregate output. The regression compares full-array and streaming results, including KPI totals, absent/current status masks, room coverage, switch anomalies, and export summaries; it now builds a separate sparse baseline index for each current chunk and checks candidates at later global baseline positions. Static Dashboard contracts were updated to enforce the streamed path. `pnpm check` passed syntax, lint, parity registry validation, portable HTML syntax, archive build and ZIP verification (225 entries); its test run then exposed an outdated static assertion about the previous baseline branch. That assertion was updated; `tests/build-config.test.js` passes all 140 tests and the final `pnpm check` passes all 1,078 tests, syntax checks, lint, parity validation, portable build, and release ZIP verification. Browser smoke remains unrun under the user-selected code-only restriction, and parity item #61 stays partial.
## Bound diagnostic samples for corrupted Final snapshots — 2026-10-19

`runBrowserDiagnostics()` previously retained a string for every corrupt Final even though the report displayed only five examples. It now increments an exact corruption count and keeps at most five example strings, so corrupt metadata cannot make diagnostics memory grow with the number of broken snapshots. `tests/diagnostics.test.js` removes chunks from six disposable Final snapshots and verifies the exact total and five-example bound. The check remains read-only and does not alter the active snapshot pointer.

The five example strings now also have a 300-character limit, preventing a malformed or legacy snapshot name from making the bounded sample itself arbitrarily large. The disposable IndexedDB regression includes a 5,000-character name and verifies the complete diagnostic remains bounded.

## Persisted history log column widths — 2026-10-19

The Next global History log previously allowed users to show or hide columns but did not retain per-column widths. The History column controls now save bounded 80–600 px widths in IndexedDB and render every MAC group's table with the same selected widths. Unit regressions cover invalid/out-of-range values, width controls, table colgroups, and app persistence wiring. Real-browser validation remains open under the selected code-only check mode.

The History controls also expose a reset-to-default action, matching the reference's reset-width operation; the wiring regression checks that it persists and rerenders the defaults.

History column controls now include an auto-width action that cursor-scans the complete filtered History result once, clamps each selected column to 80–600 px, leaves hidden columns unchanged, and persists the resulting widths without retaining the result rows. Regression covers both calculation and UI wiring.

The History log now includes one-click expand/collapse controls for all MAC groups on the current page, matching the reference's group actions. Browserless regression verifies both controls update every rendered group.

The global History log now supports confirmed deletion of every event matching the active filters, in addition to deleting checked rows on the visible page. IndexedDB streams the filtered History cursor and resolves Inventory context only when the filter requires it; deletion is one transaction across the full match set and does not load all matching IDs into memory. A regression deletes 115 matching rows spanning more than one 100-row UI page and confirms nonmatching rows remain.

On first opening, the global History filters now default to the PyQt dialog's inclusive last-30-calendar-day window and full-day time bounds. The local-date helper is covered at a month boundary; users can still change or clear the filter values.

## Bounded memory for batch-pair planning — 2026-10-19

`pairBatchEntries()` previously retained every allowed Main-to-SmartRoom/DDIO
edge before assigning files, so dense date ranges used O(Main×candidate) memory.
Pairing now sorts candidate metadata once and walks each Main's eligible neighbors
in nearest-first order using a lazy iterator. The matching heap and augmenting-path
search retain only linear-size state. Tests cover maximum cardinality against an
independent matcher over 40 deterministic graphs, dense 300×300 pairing, and a
static guard against rebuilding the Cartesian edge matrix. The dense regression
now completes in about 13 ms in Node; UI E2E remains open under the code-only
verification choice.

## Stale File System Access permission handles — 2026-10-19

`queryPermission()` can reject for a persisted directory handle that is no longer
usable. The permission helper now maps that failure to `unavailable` so startup
continues and the header offers a fresh folder picker. Unit tests cover the
rejected permission query and the fallback control state; a wiring regression
ensures archive restore remains before active-Final recovery. Real-browser
verification remains open under the selected code-only check mode.

## Heap reserve checks at streamed import boundaries — 2026-10-19

`readTableRowBatches()` now checks the browser JavaScript heap reserve before
handing each batch to its consumer. Full CSV reads and legacy XLS decoding also
estimate capacity before `arrayBuffer()`; full XLSX/XLS table readers recheck
the reserve while accumulating rows. Tests inject exhausted heap telemetry and
confirm rejection before full-file reads or delivery of a CSV batch. This does
not remove legacy XLS workbook materialization or full result arrays; those
remain open in parity row 550. Browser smoke remains open under the code-only
check choice.

## Reduce retained data in multi-file pair analysis — 2026-10-19

Multi-file pair analysis now drops row-number lists and diagnostic samples that
the comparison UI does not display, then compacts device records to fields used
by the comparison. Streaming and fallback readers use the same options. The
existing compact-analysis regression compares full and compact results, while
an app-wiring assertion covers both paths. Full result device arrays remain an
open memory cost in parity row 550.

## Avoid loading XLS worksheet cells to list sheet names — 2026-10-19

The legacy XLS sheet selector previously parsed and retained the complete
workbook even though the selector only needs its sheet names. It now asks the
local XLSX runtime for `bookSheets` metadata, verifies the returned workbook has
no materialized `Sheets`, and loads cell data only when a selected sheet is
actually read. The existing real `.xls` fixture regression also confirms
preview, full reads, dates, and streamed batches remain unchanged. Full selected
workbook parsing is still required by the legacy XLS runtime and remains open in
parity row 550. When sheet names were already loaded for the selector, an invalid
sheet selection now fails from that metadata before parsing worksheet cells;
the regression verifies no second workbook read occurs.

## User-mode bulk history deletion gate — 2026-10-19

The user-mode click gate covered deleting selected Final-history events but
omitted bulk deletion by filter and the equivalent deletion controls for
imported SQLite history shown on the accessible History screen. All four
controls are now included in the same capture-phase guard, which prevents their
handlers from running without an active engineering session. The engineering-
mode wiring regression enumerates the destructive selectors. Browser
interaction remains open under the code-only verification choice.

## Gate imported automation on engineering session — 2026-10-19

Imported SQLite schedules were being restored at application startup without an
engineering session, and their run/toggle/queue controls are rendered inside
the user-accessible History view. The UI gate now blocks those controls and the
queue file picker; handler-level checks protect task creation, queue updates,
manual/background runs, and result persistence. Scheduler restoration is a
no-op in user mode, logout/expiry stops restored schedulers, and a successful
engineer login restores them. Wiring regressions cover the controls and lifecycle
guards. Browser interaction remains open under the code-only check choice.

The same audit found that queue files could be downloaded without an engineer
login, and in-flight queue operations could outlive the session between awaits.
Queue-file download now requires an active session both at entry and immediately
before delivery. Queue mutations and scheduler restoration recheck session state
after async reads and before writes/starts. Wiring regressions cover these guards;
real-browser interaction remains open under the selected code-only check mode.
Scheduler restoration also checks the session between task pages, queue pages,
and queue items, so an expired session stops the scan promptly.

The role gate was extracted into `src/ui/engineering-access.js` and the capture-
phase app listeners now call that module. New unit tests exercise the actual
policy decisions for schedule run/toggle/add/download actions, public versus
engineering views, and restricted form events; app wiring tests confirm the
same helpers guard the real handlers.

The accessible History view previously rendered legacy schedule actions and
delete buttons while the user role was active, even though the capture gate
prevented them from running. The renderer now omits schedule/file actions for
users, role rendering hides history deletion controls, and the log refreshes
when login/logout changes role. Render and app-wiring regressions cover the
visible controls for both roles. Browser interaction remains open under the
code-only check choice.

## Overall parity progress — 2026-10-19

The active parity goal has 187 registered requirements. The release-readiness
check reports 151 still open, so 36 are currently closed: **19.3% verified
parity** and **80.7% remaining**. This is a registry-based completion measure,
not a claim that the whole application is 19.3% feature-complete by code volume.
The GitHub release remains blocked until all required rows and release checks
are closed.

The latest history-role regression removes selection checkboxes as well as
mutating actions from user-mode History, while preserving row selection for
engineering sessions. Role changes re-render both loaded history tables. The
focused regressions passed. The latest `pnpm check` passed all 1,101 tests,
portable HTML syntax checks, and the 227-entry ZIP verification. The standalone
HTML and ZIP were written to the isolated parity-check output directory; ZIP
SHA-256 is recorded in the generated sidecar for each check.
`pnpm check:release-readiness` now reports 151/187 requirements open. Browser
smoke remains open under the user's code-only verification choice.

On engineering-session exit, selected rows in both History tables are now
cleared, the global-history selected count is reset, and its selected-delete
button is disabled. This prevents stale delete selections from reappearing at
the next login. The app-wiring regression checks both selection sets and the
visible counter/button state; focused role/render tests pass.

## Legacy SQLite activity summaries in History — 2026-10-19

Imported `history_loads`, `analysis_history`, and `performance_metrics` already
had typed records and cursor paging, but their History rows omitted the useful
summary fields. The renderer now shows load counts (devices, new vendors/models,
and history enrichment), analysis device count/strategy/processing time, and
performance operation/duration. It does not expose the raw performance `details`
field. A render regression checks exact formatting and HTML escaping for
untrusted imported filenames and strategy values. For `history_loads` and
`analysis_history`, both filename columns now show only the basename, so local
directory paths are not displayed; the regression covers Windows and POSIX
paths.

## Overall parity progress — 2026-10-19

The active parity goal tracks completion against the 187 numbered requirements
in `PARITY_REGISTRY.md`. A requirement counts only when its status is fully
closed; partial and open rows count as remaining. Current verified progress is
36/187, or 19.3% (151 rows remain open or partial). Recalculate this value when
the matrix changes or a requirement is closed.

## SQLite import row-loss diagnostics — 2026-10-19

The SQLite importer previously skipped supported-table rows whose contents
could not be mapped into Next, without including those rows in the import
summary. It now counts failed row mappings in a bounded `skippedRows` object by
source table, including invalid device rows inside imported snapshots and
invalid IP mappings. The import status displays these counts alongside skipped
tables; valid rows continue through the existing additive transaction. A
regression imports a mixed valid/invalid `app_settings` set and checks both the
count and preserved setting. This improves import transparency but does not
close parity for unsupported tables, credentials, external notification
delivery, or browser smoke.

SQLite inventory normalization also now carries all recognized alternate MAC
aliases into Next's bounded `alternateMacs` field, removes duplicates of the
primary MAC, and promotes the first valid alternate to primary MAC when the
legacy row lacks a primary value. The importer regression covers alias
normalization, de-duplication, and that fallback. The same mapper preserves
the source `device_name` plus Next-supported physical address, location
hierarchy, and normalized bounded possible-IP evidence from a resolved-device
payload. The regression checks those fields alongside identity. This keeps the
strong identity tokens and device context available to comparison and
enrichment.

A disposable IndexedDB integration regression imports a `resolved_device_inventory`
row through the SQLite mapper and verifies that identity aliases, device name,
location fields, and normalized possible-IP evidence survive in Inventory.
The same end-to-end path now checks `fieldSources`, source names, and conflict
arrays from the JSON payload. The mapper accepts both an already parsed array
and a JSON string array for conflicts, caps imported conflict and source lists,
and retains the conflict-truncation marker.

Custom field provenance now survives the same import: labeled legacy custom
fields map their source to the stable Next column key, while an existing
`custom_<uuid>` key is retained instead of being hashed again. Unit coverage
checks both key forms, and disposable IndexedDB import verifies the value and
source after persistence.
