## PyQt settings migration safety regression — 2026-10-19

The PyQt settings migration regression now imports the supported `ip_enrichment_settings` booleans into the browser profile. During Main/single-file enrichment, exact local switch-IP mappings fill blank addresses; optional IPv4 `/24` matching is accepted only when all rules in that subnet agree on one address. Existing addresses remain unless the user explicitly enables overwrite. This applies before historical address inference, so matching manual rules take precedence and prevent an unnecessary history lookup. Unit and pipeline regressions cover snake-case settings, exact and partial matches, subnet conflicts, disabled enrichment, explicit overwrite, and source-address preservation. Absolute `ip_address_mapping_file` paths and automatic startup detection remain unimported; `comparison_fields`/`threshold` still cannot weaken strong device identity. A new source-trace test confirms that the PyQt controls feed weighted fuzzy scores into automatic row merging, pinning why these settings must remain excluded from Next identity resolution. Requirement #674 remains partial.

## PyQt guide wording — 2026-10-19

The database-transfer guide documents IndexedDB profile storage, `.madb.json` backup, `mac-analyzer-data.madb` folder sync, and the legacy SQLite source paths. The PyQt settings description now names vendor/model preferences and IP-based address filling; supported IP enrichment booleans are imported and applied during enrichment. Absolute mapping-file paths and automatic startup detection are not restored. A static UI regression in `tests/build-config.test.js` checks the guide wording. Requirement #674 remains partial.

## Inventory column shortcut in the guide — 2026-10-19

The guide now lists Ctrl+Shift+C as the Inventory column settings shortcut and labels it engineer-only. The existing shortcut handler already enforces that role; the build-config regression checks both the handler gate and guide wording (142/142 focused tests passed). Requirement #675 remains partial pending complete PyQt column-manager parity and browser verification.

## Error banner retry regression — 2026-10-19

Added a behavioral regression for the reported error-banner issue: after dismissal, repeated background reports with the same message stay hidden; clearing suppression at the start of a new operation allows that same error to be shown again. The focused error-banner suite passes 13/13. Requirement #677 remains partial because real browser interaction was intentionally left open under the code-only check choice.

## Strict review of completed status — 2026-10-19

Requirement #676 stated that browser export smoke remains open while its status was `Выполнено`. Per AGENTS.md, unverified capabilities stay open. Reclassified #676 as `Частично`: analytics summary XLSX logic and unit tests are present, but the real export interaction has not been verified under the code-only check choice. Current matrix progress is therefore 36/187 fully closed (19.3%), with 151 open or partial. The prior full release-readiness count was 150 before this reclassification; no application code changed in this audit.

## Reference archive current-analysis preservation — 2026-10-19

Full archives now also transfer MAC Analyzer Pro's `localVendorMappings` and `localModelMappings`: the importer parses each object as a stream and stages at most 500 OUI/MAC5 rules per batch; export cursor-reads user rules and excludes the built-in IEEE OUI dataset. Restoration is additive, so colliding local rules win. Regressions cover both directions, collisions, and the 500/1 batch boundary. Final `pnpm check` passed 1,105/1,105 tests, ESLint, 187-row registry validation, portable app/worker compilation, and the 227-entry ZIP verifier (20,185,663 bytes; SHA-256 `85ab45dc6a2ab6125ea892cde8b300eb505fe16f0471b514876b778c2c369eb5`). The HTML was inspected for these paths; SHA-256 `802B59A1A54B9357A8133554B3762C3E17EE8EED5EBF2016A53C304122D31440`. Release readiness still reports 151/187 open.

The reference archive's `workspace.ipMappings` is structurally compatible with Next's manual switch-IP→address rules. The reader now normalizes and additively merges those mappings, and Next's reference-format exporter includes its own mappings so the round trip preserves them. Tests cover reference import while retaining local rules and export/import round trip. After this addition, `pnpm check` passed 1,104/1,104 tests, ESLint, 187-row registry validation, portable HTML/worker compilation, and ZIP verification (227 entries, SHA-256 `10f2ed26b6ed61efe00990a66edafa46a24d1520fff8e6b067fc5d904957d5ab`). The built HTML was inspected for both `ipMappings` and `mac-analyzer-devices-json`; SHA-256 `52F962884267F25A0ABC77836C0242BF84820CD2F27E531B12A7CA0967126758`. `git diff --check` passed with only line-ending notices. Release readiness still has 151/187 open.

The source application's second archive-like export, `mac-analyzer-devices-json` v2, was also missing from the archive manager's accepted formats. Next now validates and streams its `devices` array into additive Inventory records, preserving the active Final. Reader and disposable IndexedDB integration tests passed for normalized fields, Inventory merge, and unchanged active Final. Requirement #128 remains partial because full restore semantics, other formats, and browser smoke are still open.

The subsequent full `pnpm check` passed syntax, ESLint, registry validation (187 unique rows), portable HTML/worker compilation, release ZIP verification (227 entries), and 1,102/1,102 tests. The generated autonomous HTML was inspected for the new reference-analysis import branches; its SHA-256 is `DFA149F35703BC56B26224864B735B91A218244F8E4ED72506ED70047696AE5E`. `git diff --check` returned 0 (only Git's LF→CRLF notices). `pnpm check:release-readiness` correctly remains blocked by 151/187 open parity requirements. Browser smoke remains intentionally unrun under the user's code-only verification choice.

Requirement #128 exposed a data-loss gap: importing MAC Analyzer Pro's v2 full archive preserved Final snapshots, Inventory, History and selected settings, but skipped its separate `currentDevices` and `invalidRows` collections. The stream reader now stages both collections in fixed batches and reconstructs them as a separate Next single-file analysis, keeping the current analysis distinct from Final history. The analysis ID is stable for repeat imports of the same exported timestamp and row counts, so the additive restore does not duplicate it. Tests cover field mapping, invalid-row preservation, no whole-file reads, repeat-import idempotence, and the unequal 501-device/two-error case across the 500-row chunk boundary. Focused importer/database tests passed (importer 59/59; database 164/164 including the chunk case). Requirement #128 remains `Частично`: source replacement semantics still differ from Next's safe additive restore, and browser smoke remains open.

The MAC Analyzer Pro full archive also carries compatible Dashboard query, vendor, room and status filters plus its comparison mode and date bounds. Next now imports those values through its normal settings validation and exports them back in the reference archive format; legacy snapshot IDs are intentionally not treated as valid Next snapshot selections. Focused settings/database checks passed. The complete `pnpm check` passed syntax, ESLint, registry validation (187 unique rows), portable HTML and worker compilation, ZIP verification (227 entries, 20,190,175 bytes, SHA-256 `c79b157198e22d22d72499b7ff776262f9e2c92ac7736bc0157c9bb447401e2f`), and 1,105/1,105 tests. ## Reference archive current analysis export — 2026-10-19

The v2 full-archive importer preserved the source app's `currentDevices` and
`invalidRows` as a chunked single-file analysis, but the reverse exporter always
emitted an empty `invalidRows` array and used active Final devices. The exporter
now selects the most recently processed single-file analysis when available,
streams its device and invalid-row chunks independently into `currentDevices`
and `invalidRows`, and falls back to active Final when no single analysis exists.
It validates chunk counts and reads only one stored chunk at a time. A disposable
IndexedDB round-trip regression verifies 501 devices and 503 invalid rows across
the 500-row boundary while confirming active Final selection is unchanged.

## Reference workspace column layout — 2026-10-19

The reference `FullJsonReport.safeState` includes `visibleColumns`,
`columnOrder`, and `ouiLength`. Next previously skipped these workspace fields.
The importer now parses each string array with a 512-entry bound, maps the
reference `macFormatted`, `vendor`, `serialNumber`, and single `oui` aliases to
Next's Inventory column catalog, and persists the resulting
`inventoryColumnConfig`. The exporter writes the compatible layout back and
chooses the visible OUI length when more than one local OUI column exists.
Import/export round-trip tests and malformed/oversized preference regressions
pass. Full report settings such as history-enrichment toggles and source file
summaries remain open pending a behavior mapping into Next.

The reference workspace also carries four `historyEnrichmentSettings` booleans.
Next validates and stores those four fields under `historyEnrichmentSettings`,
reads the earlier namespaced key as a compatibility fallback, and writes them
back on reference export. The four choices are also
available in Settings and feed both normal merge and single-file enrichment:
history can be disabled, placed before or after local OUI/MAC5 rules, and its
OUI and MAC5 prefix methods can be controlled independently. History still only
fills blank source fields. Tests cover both precedence modes, disabled lookup,
the settings passed to the indexed loader, and settings import/export.

The `workspace.files` array now streams through import and export as bounded
metadata summaries: ID, name, role, sheet, dates, row count, and column mapping.
The importer caps this at 500 summaries and 128 mappings per summary and drops
the source path and file contents. Round-trip and safety-limit regressions pass.
This does not rehydrate the original imported files or restore their data into
an active workspace, so behavior parity for saved workspace files remains open.

The Next Final history now also offers an explicit engineer-only action to
restore retained `originalFiles` to the Main/SmartRoom/DDIO merge queue. The
action validates and parses every saved file before replacing any current
selection, requests confirmation before replacing queued files, preserves the
saved role and mapping preferences, and does not start a merge or write to the
database. Core regressions cover role ordering, exact byte sizes, malformed
files, and an expired engineering session. Legacy workspace summaries that do
not include file bytes still cannot rehydrate those source files; browser
interaction remains open under code-only verification.

The legacy SQLite import instructions now distinguish the packaged `data/legacy`

## Verified transfer progress — 2026-10-20

The source `DropAreaListWidget` capability is now mapped as requirement #679.
Main, SmartRoom, and DDIO cards accept one supported dropped file and route it
through the same local import handler as the file picker. Unit checks cover
non-file drags, accepted formats, rejection of multiple or unsupported files,
dragenter/dragleave depth and drag-state cleanup; portable UI wiring is also checked. Real browser drop
smoke remains open under the user's code-only verification choice.

The current matrix contains 188 requirements: 36 are fully verified (**19.1%**)
and 152 (**80.9%**) remain open or partial. Full `pnpm check` passed 1,159 tests,
lint, 188-row parity validation, portable HTML/worker compilation, and release
ZIP verification (238 files). The rebuilt portable HTML passed the artifact
syntax check (SHA-256
`1A916543C777C2AF376AC97FACEB4845B4B743C0D5768AC915D7DC08D9AF40DA`). Batch
file assignment now maximizes valid matches and then minimizes total date
distance with linear working memory; deterministic small cases match a
brute-force optimum, and a dense 1,000-file case remains one-to-one. The prior
greedy matching counterexample is covered by regression. Both
full and standalone enrichment now stop with a named error when an active
Previous Final cannot be read; the regression verifies that only an absent
active pointer means there is no baseline. Active snapshot accessors propagate
transient IndexedDB read failures while retaining the pointer; stale or corrupt
snapshot references remain unavailable. Column mapping now mirrors the PyQt
AI detector's 100-row window and first 50 non-null scoring values, including
its keyword/pattern formula, Python `re.match` start-of-value semantics,
weights and strict thresholds; null cells are skipped before the 50-value cap,
while string placeholders are removed after it. Next also retains its
uniqueness and strong-identity guards. The review dialog separately exposes
PyQt recommendations above 15% while keeping stricter initial auto-mapping.
Custom-field source columns remain reserved when these suggestions are applied.
Regressions cover evidence in rows
81–100, the 50-value cap and the MAC score boundary; import previews remain
bounded at 100 rows. The in-app guide
and storage guide now document the Web reference database path
`data/databases/mac_analyzer_web.db`, both environment-variable overrides,
offline copying after server shutdown, and the additive local SQLite import into
Next; a static regression protects these steps. The rebuilt portable HTML was
inspected for the path and override markers (SHA-256
`2BF1E56852BDB2761A9FB38FFA1B846B0E894682F69C1920633D3D46EFF34234`). Release
readiness still fails by design while the 152 requirements remain open; no
GitHub release or push was made.

## Read-only local SQLite source verification — 2026-10-20

Re-ran the production SQLite importer against the four adjacent legacy source
files (`mac_history.db`, `vendor_model_history.db`,
`persistent_vendor_history.db`, and `mac_analyzer_stats.db`) using in-memory
SQLite and disposable IndexedDB instances. All four passed SQLite integrity
checks; every recognized table was reported, with no skipped tables, invalid
rows, or importable records in these current copies. The source files were read
only and no equipment records were printed or added to the repository. This
confirms these copies contain no legacy rows awaiting transfer; it does not
verify browser interactions or future user-provided databases.

The current portable artifact's SHA-256 is
`9DA2BB6CB24DF2851F88EA30AF7B8067064229CEF855DEDFEAD00CDF1116BD1B`; the
portable artifact checker compiled both the application and analytics worker.

## PyQt settings migration audit — 2026-10-20

The local `mac_analyzer_settings.json` has no saved file summaries and no
custom-column definitions. Empty `files` and `custom_columns` collections now
remain out of both the applied and ignored lists instead of appearing as lost
settings; the populated `visible_columns` and supported enrichment preferences
are recognized by the migration. The audit read field names and collection
shapes only, not setting values. Secret/API settings, absolute file paths,
engineering mode, comparison heuristics, and window geometry remain excluded
by their existing security, identity, or platform rules. The focused PyQt
settings suite passes all 12 tests.

## Existing MAC Analyzer Pro Web database dry-run — 2026-10-20

The existing `data/databases/mac_analyzer_web.db` is 373,112,832 bytes; its
read-only `quick_check(1)` passed. A streaming dry-run drained the production
`importLegacySQLiteFile` row mapper over the actual database via a read-only
SQLite row iterator and aggregated counts without retaining or printing device
records. It found 22 tables, of which 19 are recognized for import; the three
log/credential tables are intentionally excluded. The mapper accepted 200,023
Inventory rows, 40,161 OUI rules, 401 MAC5 rules, 4 History events, 1,001,099
legacy observations, 387 settings, one IP mapping, and one SmartRoom mapping;
`skippedRows` was empty. This validates the current database schema and row
conversion paths but does not by itself exercise IndexedDB persistence or UI
interaction. The source database remained read-only and no user records were
added to the portable package.

The same production mapper was re-run on 2026-10-22 against the local
`Mac_analyzer_Pro/data/databases/mac_analyzer_web.db` copy with native SQLite
opened read-only. It again passed integrity and streamed 200,023 Inventory
rows, 40,161 OUI rules, 401 MAC5 rules, 4 History events, 1,001,099 legacy
observations, 387 settings, one IP mapping, and one SmartRoom mapping with no
skipped rows and batches no larger than 128. The mapper-only sink retained only
aggregate counters and did not write IndexedDB.

A bounded end-to-end sample followed on 2026-10-22. The production mapper read
the entire same source, while a capped event sink passed at most ten records
per collection to a uniquely named disposable `Database`/IndexedDB instance.
The commit stored 10 Inventory, 10 OUI, 10 MAC5, 10 legacy observations, all 4
History events, and 10 imported settings (plus the required active-snapshot
pointer). The active Final remained empty, the disposable database was deleted,
and the source SQLite connection stayed read-only. This proves the real-row
conversion path reaches an IndexedDB commit for a bounded sample; it does not
prove persistence of the full 1.2-million-record import.

`tests/sqlite-importer.test.js` now also generates 100,000 settings rows lazily,
drains the real importer event stream without retaining rows, and asserts that
all records are emitted in batches no larger than 128. The focused SQLite
importer suite passes 39 tests.

## Strong-ID SQLite Inventory reconciliation — 2026-10-20

The read-only streaming dry-run exposed overlapping rows in the legacy
`device_inventory` and `resolved_device_inventory` tables. The importer now
links them only through an unambiguous strong-identity match and reports linked,
ambiguous, and conflicting counts in the import status. Duplicate stable IDs
are restored sequentially within each bounded archive page so concurrent
IndexedDB reads cannot discard fields from one source. The regression uses a
disposable IndexedDB database to verify the one-row merged result and confirms
that conflicting device IDs remain separate. An additional branch regression
confirms that duplicate resolved identities remain separate and increment the
ambiguous count; a build wiring regression checks that all three counts appear
in the user-facing import status. The SQLite importer suite passes 40 tests.

The fifth saved Dashboard chart, presence churn, now participates in PNG, HTML,
and XLSX exports. XLSX includes all churn rows, while the PNG renderer traverses
compact source rows without cloning the full value array and expands its layout
to three chart rows when all five charts are enabled. Tests cover exact churn
columns, HTML escaping, chart visibility, and 100,000 plotted rows.

Data quality now uses the shared `normalize.text()` empty-marker rules. Vendor
labels such as `Неизвестный вендор`, model placeholders, `не заполнено`, `нет
данных`, and dash markers no longer inflate field coverage; a regression checks
vendor/model/address/IP/room/port coverage and issue counts.

Room-history carry-forward now uses the same empty-marker normalization and its
compact baseline retains vendor/model confidence and source aliases. A regression
restores those fields over placeholder values and verifies their compact roster
copy.

Full `pnpm check` passed syntax checks, ESLint, 188-row registry validation,

scheduled-analysis memory release, bounded SHA-256 preflight, analytics

`BCC135A51AB544CBF15C54B15046BCF34419ACC5EC9FECC0493AA05A09C4C186`; the full

Post-build inspection of the actual standalone HTML confirmed that it contains

Enrichment now rejects a non-empty Main input when every row lacks a strong
device identity, and rejects pre-normalized Main lists containing any identity-

Vendor detection now also recognizes the legacy PyQt `device_name` text field;

records this coverage while retaining its partial status for open backend
precedence and browser-smoke requirements.

OUI enrichment now records the same 0.95 confidence as the PyQt detector, with
both `vendorSource` and `fieldSources.manufacturer` set to `OUI`. Device
normalization preserves imported `vendor_confidence`, `vendor_source`, and the
corresponding model fields as canonical properties; confidence is bounded to
0–1. Focused enrichment and normalization regressions cover the values. When a
blank manufacturer or model is restored from Previous Final, the merge also
carries its confidence and original source aliases while `fieldSources` records

The persistence regression also writes two successive Finals into a unique
disposable IndexedDB, reopens the database, and verifies that Inventory retained
both vendor/model confidence and source while the newer Final remained empty.

## Inventory search result actions — 2026-10-20

PyQt's `on_item_double_click` offers MAC history or single-device analytics for
a MAC result, and opens model-prefix details for a model result without a MAC.
Next previously opened device analytics for every double-click. Inventory nowoffers the same MAC history/analytics choice and routes a model-only result to
model-prefix analytics; Enter still opens device analytics. Unit coverage checks
MAC priority, model fallback, and empty model markers; UI-wiring coverage checks
the dialog actions. Requirement #475 remains partial until a permitted browser
smoke verifies the interactions.

## Portable archive collection loss — 2026-10-20

The native archive exporter writes `legacyObservations` and
`dataQualityReports`, but the streamed JSON reader's allow-list omitted both.
Consequently, a valid archive import silently skipped these sections. The
reader now parses both as bounded record batches. Exporter and importer share
the same ordered record-collection manifest in `src/core/portable-archive.js`.
A reader regression confirms the
emitted events, and a disposable IndexedDB integration starts with the actual
`exportArchiveStream`, then restores and reads both collections through
`importArchiveEvents`. Requirement #128 remains
partial because broader legacy restore semantics and browser smoke are still
open.

Local scheduled queue files stored as Blob values in `fileHandles` now transfer
through an optional `legacyTaskQueueFiles` root collection in 192 KiB Base64
chunks. Import checks queue/task/filename association, chunk lengths, and total
size, stages the byte chunks in IndexedDB, then stores the restored Blob and
queue reference atomically. Regressions exercise a small MIME-typed file and an
actual file above 30 MiB through `exportArchiveStream`, the portable reader,
and a separate disposable IndexedDB. The large transfer preserves edge bytes
without materializing the full Base64 payload. The full code-only check passes
all 1,192 tests, and the release gate remains blocked at 37/188.

The compatibility object-based `Database.importArchive()` path now restores
the same queue attachments. Before writing, it validates the referenced queue
row, task ID, filename, chunk boundaries, and exact byte count. Queue row and
Blob are committed together with additive collision handling. Its disposable
IndexedDB regression verifies the round-trip and confirms a mismatched task ID
is rejected before any records are written.

Portable export also preserves queue files retained inside an imported SQLite
source. It resolves that source by the task namespace and reads Base64 in bounded
chunks through the SQLite reader before writing the standard archive chunks. If
the source or reader is unavailable, export fails with an explicit error rather
than silently producing an incomplete archive. A disposable IndexedDB regression
checks attachment restoration and the missing-reader failure; browser smoke and
the remaining #128 archive checks stay open.

Named local archives now snapshot each retained legacy queue attachment into
their own 192 KiB Blob chunks. `exportLocalArchiveStream` reads the captured
queue metadata and chunks from the named archive instead of reading live queue
rows, so later queue edits cannot produce a mixed-time export. A disposable
IndexedDB regression edits both the live row and bytes after archive creation
and confirms the exported archive restores the original filename and bytes.
Local restoration also checks for a real Blob behind the saved `localBlobKey`;
the regression deletes that live Blob and queue row and verifies both are
recovered from the archive.

The bundled IEEE payload now has a searchable local CID catalog in the data
settings. It searches Assignment prefixes and organization names, pages the
complete CID list, escapes organization labels, and never adds CID assignments
to MAC vendor lookup. Unit and app-wiring regressions cover these boundaries.

## Previous Final vendor/model provenance — 2026-10-20

The compact Previous Final projection omitted `vendorConfidence`, `vendorSource`,
`modelConfidence`, `modelSource`, and `fieldSources`. Blank Main fields could
receive the prior value while losing its confidence and original inference
source. The projection now retains those fields until merge completes, then the
existing comparison trim releases them. A disposable IndexedDB regression writes
a Final, reloads its chunk through `loadCompactSnapshot`, enriches blank Main
fields, and verifies both confidence values, both original source values, and
per-field `Previous Final` provenance. The focused regressions, lint, all 1,191
tests, portable syntax/build, and ZIP verification pass. Browser smoke remains
open under the selected code-only verification mode.
## IndexedDB portable transfer policy — 2026-10-20

A disposable-schema regression now classifies every one of the 24 IndexedDB
stores. Durable record collections and snapshot/original-file stores use the
portable archive; named local archives have their own export flow; file handles
remain origin-bound; analytics caches, scratch queries, and prefix-count indexes
are rebuildable. The test fails if a new store is introduced without an explicit
transfer policy. The policy assertion passes; this does not replace the open
browser smoke requirements for archive workflows.
## Whitespace-separated MAC input — 2026-10-20

PyQt's `MACValidator.normalize` removes formatting separators before parsing.
Next now accepts the standard space-separated six-byte form and the common
three-group four-hex form, in addition to colon, dash, Cisco-dot and compact
forms. Source-field normalization now follows PyQt's exact strip-and-pad rule,
including arbitrary non-hex characters, compact values, and visible all-zero/
broadcast values. Those placeholders remain rejected as strong identity
evidence by the separate strict normalizer, so they cannot merge records. A
source-linked regression in `tests/legacy-pyqt-settings.test.js` checks the
reference cleaner and compares both conversion paths; behavior coverage is also
in `tests/normalize.test.js`. Browser smoke for import remains open under #658.
An earlier checkpoint passed 1,192 tests, lint, portable build, and archive
verification; that historical result does not certify the current revision.
## SQLite resolved Inventory confidence provenance — 2026-10-20

`legacyDevice()` rebuilt resolved inventory payloads from an allow-list but
omitted vendor/model confidence and explicit source fields. Those values were
silently lost on SQLite → IndexedDB import, even though `payload_json` held them.
The importer now preserves camelCase and snake_case aliases and clamps finite
confidence to 0–1. Unit and disposable IndexedDB regressions verify the values
and source strings survive through `inventoryPage`. Requirement #653 remains
partial for its remaining SQLite and runtime checks.

## SQLite snapshot confidence provenance — 2026-10-20

The same SQLite device mapper also feeds imported Final snapshot chunks. A
streamed SQLite snapshot regression now verifies that vendor/model confidence
and source filenames survive that path as well as Inventory import. All 40
focused SQLite importer tests pass; the full code check passes 1,192 tests,
lint, portable HTML syntax, and ZIP verification. Requirement #653 remains
partial until its outstanding SQLite/runtime checks are resolved.

## SQLite table schema coverage — 2026-10-20

An audit of every table recognized by the SQLite importer found that
`mac_movements` was the only supported table without required-column
validation. Its converter requires `mac` and `field_name`, and the historical
timestamp must come from either `timestamp` or `changed_at`; those constraints
are now explicit alongside the existing `mac5_model_history` schema. A
regression verifies that every supported table has a schema policy, that
incompatible movement/MAC5 tables are reported, and that the legacy `changed_at`
variant preserves its observation time while compatible settings continue to
import. The PyQt source audit parses all eight `CREATE TABLE` definitions and
checks that every required importer column exists in the actual source schema.
The focused SQLite importer suite passes 43/43; the latest full code
gate passes 1,195/1,195 tests, lint, portable build and ZIP verification.
Browser/runtime parity for requirement #653 remains open.

## Web reference index pinned — 2026-10-20

The adjacent MAC Analyzer Pro Web reference is version 1.0.82. Its
`PARITY_STATUS.json` lists 178 checked source requirements and reports complete.
All 178 IDs occur in the Next matrix; the matrix has 10 additional Next-specific
requirements. The exact index, including its source SHA-256, is pinned in
`tests/fixtures/web-parity-source-index.json`; parity-registry regressions check
that every source ID is present and each source wording matches the reviewed

proves source-index coverage, not completion of browser behavior: 151 Next

## Portable error-banner regression — 2026-10-20

The open desktop artifact was built before the current worktree build and its
embedded `bindErrorBannerDismiss` lacked the `MutationObserver` that moves an
already-visible error into a dialog opened afterward. The newer worktree source
contains that recovery path and its unit wiring test. The portable artifact
checker now requires the observer and dialog-close restoration to be present in
the generated HTML, so a stale bundle cannot pass the artifact gate. The real
browser click remains unverified under the user's code-only check choice.

## Inventory source column — 2026-10-20

The Web reference exposes source as a result column, while Next's Inventory catalog had no matching selectable field. The catalog now includes an optional “Источник” column. It displays the normalized single source value when available, then falls back to the sources list used by imported and older records. Because Inventory table and XLSX exports share the selected column configuration, the field is available to both. Unit regression covers both stored representations; browser interaction remains open under the selected code-only verification mode.


## Legacy OUI column migration — 2026-10-20

The Web result-column preference stores the generic oui key, while Next has
length-specific oui3 through oui6 fields. When the legacy preference omits
its OUI length, the source defaults to three bytes. Next now applies that same
default during migration and continues to honor explicit lengths from three
through six bytes. Regression checks that order, visibility, and width survive
conversion without leaving an unsupported generic key. A second test compares
the full 17-column Web default with the corresponding Next catalog keys.

## Paginate imported autosaves — 2026-10-21

The Data view previously rendered only the first 100 imported autosave slots.
It now pages through the IndexedDB settings cursor in groups of 50, matching
the source catalog's bounded result size without loading all settings at once.
The next cursor is restricted to the `legacyAutosave:` key range; page
navigation discards stale forward cursors when the user changes direction.
Tests verify complete ordered traversal, the final-page boundary, rejection of
foreign cursors, and UI wiring. Browser interaction remains open under the
selected code-only verification mode.

## DDIO target MAC membership memory — 2026-10-22

`buildDdIoIndexFromRows` used a JavaScript `Set` of formatted primary and
alternate MAC strings as a target filter, alongside the full enriched device
array. Replaced that duplicated string collection with an exact open-addressed
index backed by `Uint32Array` high/low words and a `Uint8Array` occupancy map.
The index grows below a 70% load factor and compares the full 48-bit address,
so collisions do not alter DDIO matching. Already-normalized MACs from the
shared identity visitor enter and query the compact index directly without a
second normalization pass. `tests/ddio-overlay.test.js` exercises 2,048 exact
targets across multiple growth steps and verifies neighboring
non-target MACs remain excluded. The full device array inside enrichment is
still held until later passes finish, so parity row #550 remains partial.

## Catch up delayed Final autosaves — 2026-10-21

Browser timers can be delayed while a tab is suspended. The five-minute XLSX
autosave now checks its elapsed-time deadline when the tab becomes visible and
runs once immediately if the interval elapsed, while preserving the original
five-minute first-save delay and avoiding duplicate overlapping writes. Unit
tests cover the exact deadline, early return, long suspension, invalid clock,
and invalid interval; build-wiring checks cover the visibility event. A real
browser timer/throttling smoke remains open.

## Include optional-host Python tests in the full check — 2026-10-21

The release check discovered every JavaScript test but omitted the Python
integration tests for optional storage. `scripts/run-python-tests.mjs` now
requires Python 3.10+ and runs `unittest discover`; `scripts/check.mjs` invokes
it after the Node suite, and the release archive checker requires the runner.
The full script passed 1,264 JavaScript tests, all 39 Python integration tests,
lint, the portable HTML build, and full ZIP verification. In this managed
worktree the `pnpm` shim refuses to replace the shared `node_modules` link, so
the same full check script was invoked directly with Node and the bundled
Python executable. Real-browser smoke remains open.

## Probe optional-host read APIs during system diagnostics — 2026-10-21

The optional-host system diagnostics endpoint now checks that `/api/health`,
`/api/storage/structure`, and `/api/database/summary` return valid response
shapes over fixed loopback GET requests. Bodies are limited to 1 MiB, inspected
in memory, and excluded from the report. The HTTP integration test covers all
three checks and confirms the SQLite file stays unchanged. Focused validation
passed 153 Node tests and all 43 Python integration tests. Browser smoke remains
open.

## Page snapshot metadata during browser diagnostics — 2026-10-21

Browser diagnostics previously read every snapshot metadata record into one
array before checking chunks. `Database.snapshotMetadataPage()` now reads a
bounded keyset page, and diagnostics processes one page of at most 100 records
at a time while retaining exact totals and active-Final validation. A disposable
IndexedDB regression checks 205 snapshots across three pages and fails if the
full `listSnapshots()` API is used. Focused validation passed 210 tests across
the diagnostics and database suites. Browser smoke remains open.

The same diagnostics flow now verifies IndexedDB write access without leaving
a record behind: it adds a unique probe key in `settings`, aborts that
transaction before commit, and confirms the key is absent in a fresh readonly
transaction. Regressions cover both a successful abort and a storage backend
that rejects readwrite transactions. The diagnostic remains non-mutating.

Corrupt single-file analysis diagnostics now cap each of five displayed
filename/error examples to 300 characters, matching Final integrity output.
The regression creates six broken analyses with long imported filenames and
checks both exact corruption totals and the bounded sample. Browser smoke remains
open.

## Full code-only gate rerun — 2026-10-21

The current revision passes `node scripts/check.mjs`: syntax checks, ESLint,
188-requirement registry validation, portable HTML and worker compilation,
portable artifact syntax inspection, all 1,281 Node tests, all 47 optional-host
Python integration tests, and full 271-entry release ZIP verification. The ZIP
verifier prints and checks the current archive size and SHA-256 rather than

open or partial, including 114 browser smoke/E2E checks, 30 backend/REST

## Streamed JSON table materialization — 2026-10-21

`readTable()` previously read a whole JSON file into a decoded string, parsed the
full object graph, and then mapped the collection into a second rows array. It
now uses the existing JSON row parser in fixed batches and appends each batch to
the single output rows array required by current analysis consumers. A focused
regression uses a File-like input whose `arrayBuffer()` throws and checks exact
headers, rows, and `rowCount`. This reduces peak duplicate representations; the
final analyzed-device arrays and other bounded-memory gaps remain open.

## Streaming JSON nesting limits — 2026-10-21

The token parser previously recursed through parsed table rows and skipped archive
metadata without a nesting ceiling. A compact untrusted JSON file could therefore
ask the parser to retain a deeply nested value. Parsing and skipping now share a
128-level limit and return a format error beyond it. Regression tests cover both
a nested table cell and metadata skipped during archive-header validation. The
limit does not close the broader bounded-memory requirement: complete output
rows and several downstream datasets still remain materialized.
Full code-only verification after this change passed 1,281 Node tests, 47 Python integration tests, the portable build, packaged Windows launcher/runtime smoke, and 271-entry ZIP verification. The archive verifier reported 34,737,855 bytes and SHA-256 `4b8c04ff86b7d7fc53b2bb2cfcac17bada4c840bbdc4af498608ca995bb50075`. The local HTTP integration test also passed on the full rerun after its test-only response timeout increased from 2 to 10 seconds; browser smoke remains open by user choice.
## Workspace source handles across browser restarts — 2026-10-21

The compact workspace checkpoint intentionally stores only bounded UI state, so
selected Main, SmartRoom, and DDIO source files were lost when the app restarted.
The existing IndexedDB `fileHandles` store now also persists origin-bound
`FileSystemFileHandle` references for those roles, plus bounded name/type/size/
modification-time metadata. A granted read permission allows restore after the
engineer enters the app; if the browser needs permission, the user must grant it
from the explicit source-card action. Changed files require confirmation. The
ordinary file input and drag/drop continue to work and clear the prior saved
handle. Handles, paths, and source contents are not exported in portable archives;
archive transfer still requires explicitly selecting the source files again.

Added `src/core/workspace-source-handles.js`, app wiring, `fileHandles` database
methods, documentation, and regressions for metadata validation, permission
checks, read-only reauthorization, archive exclusion, and UI wiring. Focused
`tests/build-config.test.js` now passes 150/150 after the static control wiring
check was updated to recognize this delegated source-card button. This remains
partial in requirement #242 until the allowed real browser smoke validates the
prompt/restart flow. No schema migration was required because the existing
`fileHandles` store is already present.

## Full code-only release gate after workspace restore changes — 2026-10-21

The complete `node scripts/check.mjs` passed: all 1,287 Node tests, all 47

`de54b4c3a929f2e016fd29101c68463eb7e125725a89707f133e132ba7c218fd`). The

## Source replacement failure safety — 2026-10-21

File replacement now parses the candidate before clearing the previously saved origin-bound handle or applying the new table. Persistent FileSystemFileHandle selection also stores its record only after a successful parse; a malformed file therefore leaves the current source selection and existing saved handle unchanged. The focused source-handle UI and permission checks pass 154/154, and the complete code-only gate passed again with 1,287/1,287 Node tests and 47/47 Python integration tests. The portable builder produced an 8,929,143-byte standalone HTML with SHA-256 `de54b4c3a929f2e016fd29101c68463eb7e125725a89707f133e132ba7c218fd`; the release archive verifier confirms 273 entries.

## Restoring mass-merge folders after restart — 2026-10-21

The mass-merge source roster held three full browser `File` lists only in memory,
so a restart discarded the selected Main, SmartRoom, and DDIO folders. Added an
optional origin-bound `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` for each role in the existing
IndexedDB `fileHandles` store, with explicit read-only permission checks and a
click-bound reauthorization path. Restoring walks nested directories, collects
only supported CSV/TSV/TXT/JSON/XLS/XLSX/XLSM files in deterministic path order,
and repopulates the review roster; it never starts a merge automatically.
Manual file selection, drag/drop, or removing a roster item deletes that role's
saved directory handle so startup cannot silently restore a stale list. Handles,
paths, and file contents stay out of portable archives.

Added `src/core/batch-directory-handles.js`, database methods, source-card controls,
portable module wiring, storage documentation, and regressions for recursive
filtering/order, permission gating, archive exclusion, and UI wiring. Focused
build-config, database, and directory-helper suites pass 386/386. The complete

now maps `src/core/batch.js` plus the importer, limits and UI modules to those

## Proxy-independent packaged-host health and shutdown — 2026-10-21

The packaged Windows launcher used `urllib` to probe and stop its own
`127.0.0.1` server. Under the host's proxy configuration, those requests did
not reliably reach loopback: the listener started, but readiness timed out, and
the stop command could leave its server process running. Health and shutdown
now use direct `HTTPConnection` requests to loopback. Startup allows up to 20
seconds for first-run one-file extraction. The Windows runtime check captures
launcher and stop output so a future failure includes its actual diagnostic.

Regressions prove health and authenticated shutdown work when
`urllib.request.urlopen` is unavailable, verify the startup timeout, and retain
the invalid-content-type response/body-framing regression. The full code-only
gate passed 1,298/1,298 Node tests and 48/48 Python integration tests, ESLint,
portable HTML and worker compilation, Windows host build and runtime
startup/shutdown, and the 275-entry archive verifier. The generated autonomous
HTML is 8,931,087 bytes, SHA-256
`f19974769ac0d008555f0121a043a2932f1fac2790110e825093408a1d33fb0e0`; the
Windows host is 13,363,186 bytes, SHA-256
`68cfe4932a218557538cb60655a2789d5af223c338925a47b36baf635a6e5fbb`.
The full ZIP verifier checked all 275 entries, including source, documentation,
the autonomous HTML and Windows host. Browser smoke remains unverified in the
selected code-only mode; release readiness is still 38/188, with 150
requirements open or partial.

## Preserve nested folder paths in the mass-merge review — 2026-10-21

The Web reference retains `webkitRelativePath` in its batch descriptors, IDs,
sorting, and review labels. Next restored files from `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`
were sorted by relative path, but `listBatchDirectoryFiles` discarded that path
when returning the `File` objects. Two files with the same basename in different
subfolders therefore looked identical in the roster and assignment controls.

The directory helper now keeps a relative path per returned `File` in a
memory-only `WeakMap`. Batch entries and their stable sort use that path, and the
file roster, pair table, assignment options, mapping headings, progress and
failure details display it. The source filename remains unchanged for date
parsing; relative paths are not written to IndexedDB or portable archives.
Directory scanning also transforms `{path, file}` records into the returned
`File` list in place, so a second full array is not held during restoration.
Regressions cover nested duplicate basenames, path-aware pair assignment, UI
wiring, and in-place list transformation. The focused suites passed 185/185.
Requirement #242 and #679 map this behavior and remain partial pending real
browser interaction checks.

## Validate optional SQLite browser page boundaries — 2026-10-21

The loopback UI now rejects snapshot/device responses whose row counts exceed
the requested page size, whose snapshot cursor does not advance to the last
listed ID, or whose device offset skips/duplicates rows relative to the current
page. Device pages must also end exactly at the declared snapshot count when no
next cursor is returned. Focused regressions cover over-limit responses,
non-advancing cursors, skipped offsets, and valid final pages. The complete
code-only gate passed 1,325 Node tests and 48 Python integration tests, ESLint,
the parity registry, portable HTML/worker compilation, Windows host build/runtime
checks, and verification of the 276-entry ZIP (34,987,142 bytes; SHA-256
`6f9038a150b97d0a89f5ca28daa75365e109930b18e5dbab82d12f5c67c78035`). The
autonomous HTML is 8,970,179 bytes; SHA-256
`483c424af326a14a000049d93af8c88632476959a8b075713a3d371b9d36f7fe`. Release
readiness remains 38/188 (20.2%); real-browser runtime smoke remains open under
the selected code-only mode.

## Prefer the closest similar-device MAC group — 2026-10-21

The Web detection index tries similar devices by 5-byte, 4-byte, then 3-byte
MAC prefixes, while the Next browser detector only used 4 bytes. A broad group
could therefore obscure a more specific consensus or produce no answer on a
tie. Next now keeps aggregate vendor/model evidence for the 5-byte and 3-byte
levels alongside its existing 4-byte map, subtracts same-MAC observations, and
selects the first level with evidence. The detector skips aggregate construction
when inference is disabled or all vendor/model values are already known.
Vendor confidence follows the Web reference (0.78/0.70/0.62); inferred model
confidence is 90% of the selected group confidence. Both pass through the user's
configured threshold; ties at the closest level remain unresolved. Regressions cover narrow-over-broad disagreement, 3-byte
fallback, same-MAC exclusion, and confidence threshold. The full code-only gate
passed 1,328 Node tests and 48 Python integration tests, ESLint, parity registry
validation, portable HTML/worker compilation, Windows host build/runtime checks,
and verification of the 276-entry ZIP. The standalone HTML is 8,972,963 bytes
with SHA-256
`993aceb7b2d4760fbb19de4605fe15ba1adc1db820d837a9dffc68f9cd923210`. Requirement
#371 remains partial for browser smoke and the external MACVendor integration.

Additional #371 parity correction (2026-10-21): the Web reference indexes model
observations even when their manufacturer is blank. Next now retains these
model-only counts at each MAC-prefix level, excludes observations from the same
normalized MAC, and infers a model only when a unique candidate clears the
confidence threshold. Vendor ties still prevent cross-vendor model selection.
Regressions cover model-only peers, same-MAC exclusion, and unresolved ties.

Additional #371 PyQt parity clarification (2026-10-21): the standalone PyQt
text extractor returns `min(2 × keyword hits / keyword count, 1.0)`, then
`detect_vendor_from_device` multiplies that candidate confidence by `0.8`
before comparing it with the configured threshold. Next matches the complete
device-detection path. The Juniper/SRX regression verifies the resulting score
at thresholds 0.5 and 0.55.

## Match PyQt history priority confidence rules — 2026-10-21

The PyQt enrichment pipeline runs OUI/model knowledge and `VendorDetector`
before `HistoryEnricher`. Without `priority_history`, history fills only fields
that remain unknown. With priority enabled, a historical value can replace a
weaker text or similar-device inference only when its confidence is higher;
source fields, OUI, and local model-prefix knowledge remain protected by the
Next product invariants. Next now performs the history lookup after local
detection in both modes and requests history for already inferred candidates
when confidence comparison is needed. `tests/enrichment.test.js` covers the
fallback, protected strong values, and replacement of weaker text inference.
`tests/legacy-pyqt-settings.test.js` anchors the ordering and confidence rule to
the root PyQt reference. The Web backend's direct detector still checks history
before OUI/text regardless of the PyQt setting, so the reference precedence
difference remains open in requirement #371.

## Match historical confidence to PyQt occurrence evidence — 2026-10-21

PyQt derives history confidence from the number of observations with
`min(0.9, 0.5 + min(occurrences, 100) * 0.004)`. Next previously used fixed
confidence values for MAC prefixes and defaulted missing per-MAC confidence to
0.93. It also filtered historical values through the text detector's threshold,
although the PyQt HistoryEnricher does not. Next now derives missing SQLite
history confidence and indexed prefix confidence from occurrence counts, keeps
explicit source confidence, and lets the existing history priority rule decide
whether to fill or replace a value. Regressions cover imported rows without
confidence, exact and prefix counts, explicit confidence preservation, and
history filling a blank field with a detector threshold of 0.98.

## Stream multi-file comparison details — 2026-10-21

The multi-file comparison screen previously retained full `added`, `absent`,
`modified`, `unchanged`, and `ambiguous` detail arrays after the core had already
computed them. It now builds a `createFinalComparisonEventPlan` with typed status
marks and matched positions. Selected comparison fields are counted by a pass
that retains no device rows; UI pages and CSV/XLSX writers regenerate each
comparison record only while consuming that page or stream. Existing status
ordering, field selection, ambiguity details, exact totals, and all-pair exports
remain covered by `tests/multi-file-comparison.test.js`. A 12,249-device regression
checks the planned final page, while the app wiring test verifies the live
multi-file flow no longer calls the array-collecting comparator.

The settings label previously claimed that history had priority over OUI/MAC5,

## Support both vendor-history precedence references — 2026-10-21

The PyQt and Web references use different vendor/model history ordering. Next now
stores a `precedenceProfile` with the history settings. The default remains
`pyqt`, preserving existing installations; selecting `web` applies legacy
history suggestions before local OUI/MAC5 and detector stages when history
priority is enabled. Explicit Main values and Previous Final values remain
protected. With priority disabled, history fills only fields left unresolved by
local detection in either profile. The choice survives SQLite settings,
portable archives, and legacy settings normalization. Regressions cover both
profiles, explicit source values, settings normalization, portable import/export,
and the settings UI. Requirement #371 still needs browser smoke and the external
MACVendor integration remains excluded by the local-data privacy rule.

## Release cached table rows during enrichment — 2026-10-21

When an already parsed table is passed to `executeEnrichment`, its rows are now
consumed in batches of at most 500. The consumed row references are cleared as
the normalized device list is built, reducing overlap between raw source rows
and output records for non-streamable formats. Closing the iterator early also
clears the remaining cached rows while preserving the first 100 preview rows; a
retry reads the retained source `File` again. `tests/importer.test.js` checks batch boundaries and early iterator
closure, and `tests/build-config.test.js` verifies production wiring. This does
not remove the full in-memory Final device list, so requirement #550 remains
partial. Focused importer and build wiring tests passed.

## Compact exact Final ID validation — 2026-10-21

Replaced the temporary `Set` used to reject duplicate Final IDs with two
`Uint32Array` buffers and open addressing. FNV-1a selects a candidate slot, but
the original ID is compared before reporting a duplicate, so hash collisions
between distinct IDs remain valid. The scan checks abort and yields every 500
records. `tests/enrichment.test.js` covers both exact duplicates and two
distinct IDs with a verified hash collision; `tests/build-config.test.js`
checks the production wiring. This supersedes the earlier `finalIds.clear()`
optimization note. Requirement #550 remains partial because the Final device
array itself is still resident.

all configured Node.js tests, 48 Python integration tests, syntax, ESLint,

SHA-256 `94a4ff473c95cc1c0f4f9b9f508bbe6314d4059927186d2b24e1be02f482862a`.

not run under the user's code-only instruction.

## Release historical address pages in single-file analysis — 2026-10-21

The standalone single-file enrichment path now sets
`retainHistoricalAddressIndex: false`. Historical switch-address evidence is
loaded for bounded device batches and applied immediately, so the complete
intermediate switch-IP map is not retained for the rest of analysis. This map
was not consumed by the single-file results UI. The existing enrichment
regression verifies exact results and 500/500/205 page boundaries in
non-retaining mode; `tests/build-config.test.js` now confirms the standalone
production wiring. The full Final device list remains in memory, so #550 stays
partial.

The complete code-only `node scripts/check.mjs` gate passed after the
single-file memory change: all configured Node.js tests, 48 Python integration
tests, syntax, ESLint, registry, portable HTML/worker, Windows backend
build/runtime, and ZIP verification. Browser smoke was not run under the user's
code-only instruction; readiness remains 38/188.

## Keep switch-address alternatives compact — 2026-10-21

Historical switch-address aggregation now totals each candidate using scalar
counters, sorts compact address summaries, and only then builds the detailed
snapshot observations for the winning address. Competing addresses retain the
same exact snapshot/device totals and ordering but no longer allocate full
observation arrays that are discarded after selecting the winner.
`tests/historical-mapping.test.js` verifies the winner evidence and 100 compact
alternatives; `tests/build-config.test.js` guards the bounded materialization
structure. Existing consensus and tie-break tests preserve decision semantics.

## Close HTTP connections after rejecting unread request bodies — 2026-10-21

The optional host now sends `Connection: close` after unsupported methods and
closes the connection after rejecting an SQLite restore. These paths can reject
before consuming the request body; keeping the HTTP/1.1 connection alive let
leftover body bytes be parsed as the next request. Python integration regressions
exercise a second request on the same `HTTPConnection` and verify the server
reconnects cleanly. The 48-test Python integration suite passes.

The complete code-only `node scripts/check.mjs` gate passed after the memory and
HTTP connection fixes: all configured Node.js tests, 48 Python integration
tests, syntax, ESLint, parity validation, portable HTML/worker checks, Windows
host build/runtime, and full archive verification. This run also confirms the
HTTP keep-alive regression is fixed. Browser smoke remains unrun by the user's
code-only instruction; release readiness remains 38/188.

## Project historical address evidence at ingestion — 2026-10-21

The switch-address accumulator now retains only the five source properties
needed to calculate counts and render evidence (`snapshotId`, `snapshotName`,
`createdAt`, `deviceCount`, `sampleMacs`). Unused properties on IndexedDB cursor
records are no longer kept alive through aggregation. The existing result
regression compares all rendered evidence fields exactly, and the build-config
regression guards the projection at the production accumulator.

The complete code-only `node scripts/check.mjs` gate passed after compacting
historical address records: all configured Node.js tests, 48 Python integration
tests, syntax, ESLint, parity registry, portable HTML/worker, Windows backend
build/runtime, and full release archive verification. The inspected standalone
HTML is 8,985,456 bytes; browser smoke remains intentionally unrun under the
user's code-only instruction. Readiness remains 38/188.

## Avoid per-device temporary arrays in DDIO target filtering — 2026-10-21

`buildDdIoIndexFromRows` now adds normalized primary and alternate MACs
straight into the shared target-membership `Set`. The previous path allocated a
spread array, a temporary `unique` result, and a temporary local `Set` for each
device even though the global set performs the needed deduplication.
`tests/ddio-overlay.test.js` verifies primary/alternate MAC and Device ID
filtering while excluding unrelated DDIO rows; `tests/build-config.test.js`
checks the allocation-free path.

The complete code-only `node scripts/check.mjs` gate passed after removing
per-device temporary allocations from DDIO target membership: all configured
Node.js tests, 48 Python integration tests, syntax, ESLint, parity registry,
portable HTML/worker, Windows host build/runtime, and archive verification. The
portable artifact is 8,985,605 bytes; the browser smoke check remains open by
user choice.

## Apply DDIO evidence without per-device MAC copies — 2026-10-21

`applyDdIoIp` now visits the device's primary MAC and alternate-MAC values
directly, avoiding a spread array and local `unique`/`Set` allocation for every
device. Possible-IP and custom-field accumulators still deduplicate their output
and keep the same bounded conflict behavior. Regressions verify repeated
normalized aliases produce exact possible IPs and one conflict event; wiring
checks guard the direct-iteration path.

The full code-only `node scripts/check.mjs` gate passed after direct DDIO MAC
iteration: all configured Node.js tests, 48 Python integration tests, syntax,
ESLint, parity registry, portable HTML/worker, Windows host build/runtime, and
release ZIP verification. The generated standalone HTML is 8,985,708 bytes;
real-browser smoke remains open under the user's code-only instruction.

## Restore explicit same-machine Final copy APIs — 2026-10-21

The optional loopback host again supports bounded, validated Inventory, History,
and Final snapshot APIs, and the browser UI can copy the active Final after a
direct user action. This keeps IndexedDB as the browser primary store and does
not add automatic sync or a cross-computer database. Snapshot uploads are
same-origin, loopback-only, capped at 256 MiB, streamed in bounded frames, and
committed atomically after row-count and strong-identity validation. Portable
archive export/import remains the transfer path between browser profiles or
computers. Older audit notes describing the intentionally removed API state are
historical; the current implementation and tests are documented in
`docs/DATA_STORAGE.md`. Real-browser smoke remains open.

## Make explicit Final copy retries idempotent — 2026-10-21

If the loopback host commits a Final but its HTTP response is lost, retrying the
same transfer now returns success when snapshot ID, normalized metadata, device
count, and content SHA-256 match. A reused ID with changed metadata or content
still returns conflict and leaves the stored snapshot intact. Python store and
HTTP regressions cover exact retry, content conflict, metadata conflict, and
atomic preservation; the UI reports when the identical copy already exists.
Before accepting a retry, the host now cursor-reads stored JSON rows and
recomputes their SHA-256, so damaged SQLite content cannot inherit trust from a
stale signature column. A regression mutates stored device JSON without updating
the signature and verifies that the retry remains a conflict.

## Copy active Final History to the optional host — 2026-10-21

After the explicit active-Final copy succeeds, the UI now streams History events
linked to that Final from IndexedDB in bounded cursor pages and posts bounded,
idempotent batches to the loopback host. The browser validates each event's
snapshot reference and verifies the host's inserted/duplicate counts. Regressions
cover pagination, request bounds, loopback-only transport, and the no-external-
upload privacy boundary. This does not close the real-browser smoke requirement.

## Copy cumulative Inventory to the optional host — 2026-10-21

The loopback UI now has a separate explicit Inventory copy action. It reads
IndexedDB cursor pages to a fixed high-water key, then sends records in requests
bounded by 500 devices and 900 KiB. Custom fields, provenance, alternate MACs,
and observation counts are preserved. The SQLite API treats a supplied
observation count as a cumulative value and takes the maximum, so retrying a
copy cannot fabricate new observations. Identity conflicts are counted and
reported to the UI. Node, SQLite-store, and privacy regressions cover the transfer;
the portable HTML and Windows host checks passed. Matrix row #625 remains partial
until the user-selected code-only limitation on browser smoke is lifted.

## Critical comparison without intermediate arrays — 2026-10-22

`compareFinals` previously mapped every modified device into a temporary cloned
row, filtered the whole result for critical changes, and retained the resulting
critical subset alongside the original modified rows. It now scans modified
rows and their field changes directly, creating a retained row only when at
least one confirmed critical field differs between two known values. A 1,205
device regression checks exact critical totals, field order, and original row
references; a source guard prevents the former `map`/`filter` allocations.
Full pair materialization and browser smoke remain open parity requirements.

## Stream Dashboard HTML export — 2026-10-22

Dashboard HTML is generated one KPI/chart row at a time. The UI streams it to
the Save File picker when available, otherwise to a backpressure-aware readable
stream and final Blob. It no longer accumulates the complete HTML or all churn
table rows as strings before saving. The previous string API remains as a
compatibility wrapper. Regression confirms byte-equivalent markup and 100,000
churn rows with individual output fragments below 2,000 characters. Browser UI
smoke remains open under the selected code-only mode.

## Dashboard summary avoids retaining critical change rows — 2026-10-22

The in-memory Dashboard summary needs exact critical-device/event counters, not
a second array of critical row objects. It now calls `compareFinals` with
`includeCriticalRows: false`; `releaseDashboardCurrentDevices` calculates the
same counts from modified rows, while detail panels continue reading bounded
pages from IndexedDB. Regression verifies exact IP/MAC critical counts and an
empty critical detail array, and a wiring guard confines the option to the
summary path. Full pairwise detail materialization and browser smoke remain
open requirements.

## Dashboard summary skips unused changed-location sets — 2026-10-22

The three production comparison passes each accumulated distinct changed-room
and changed-MAC sets even though the selected-pair summary does not consume
them; the separate history worker computes its weekly distinct counts. The
summary now opts out with `includeChangeLocations: false`. The public summary
defaults preserve the previous room/MAC output. A 1,205-device regression
confirms all 2,410 changed-field events and 1,205 critical MAC changes while
returning no unused location arrays; wiring guards prevent allocating the sets
on this path. Full comparison memory bounds and browser smoke remain open.

## Optional host Final-copy management — 2026-10-22

Added an explicit, paged UI for SQLite copies stored by the same-computer
optional host. Deleting a selected Final requires confirmation and uses one
SQLite transaction to remove that snapshot and its linked History while
preserving Inventory and other snapshots. Schema v6 adds indexed
`source_final_id`; the upgrade backs up older databases and backfills existing
event payloads in batches of 500. Python store and HTTP regressions plus the
JavaScript loopback-control tests cover the API, migration, pagination contract,
deletion scope, and preservation. Browser UI smoke was not run under the
selected code-only verification mode.

## Reduce legacy XLS cell-index overhead — 2026-10-22

Legacy `.xls` was parsed through SheetJS in object worksheet mode, allocating
an address-keyed property for each populated cell before the app could emit its
bounded row batches. The workbook parse now requests dense storage and the cell
reader accepts SheetJS's `!data` row arrays. The real legacy XLS fixture
regression confirms selected-sheet headers and values remain exact through
preview and one-row batches. File/heap preflight remains in place. SheetJS
still materializes the legacy workbook internally, so this reduces per-cell
index overhead but does not make XLS parsing constant-memory; parity item #550
stays partial.

The XLS preview path also passes SheetJS `sheetRows = maxRows + 2`, retaining
the header, requested sample and one extra data row used to mark the preview as
truncated. A real fixture test compares the bounded `!ref` to `!fullref`, while
the unbounded row-batch path still returns the entire selected sheet. This
avoids parsing every row merely to show a mapping preview; full XLS processing
remains limited by SheetJS's materialized workbook model.

After converting each legacy XLS row to its bounded string array, the reader
now releases that row from dense `!data` (and removes sparse cells in the
compatibility fallback). It releases the header after mapping it as well.
Regression checks source-row release for preview, streamed batches and full
compatibility reads while preserving exact values. This removes the overlap
between already-converted output rows and the source cell objects during row
iteration, although initial SheetJS workbook parsing is still materialized.

The streamed Final comparison now lazily creates each matched device's change
list only when a known field actually differs. Unchanged callbacks retain the
existing `changes: []` payload. A 1,205-device regression processes bounded
chunks and checks the callback contract and exact totals. This reduces
short-lived allocations on large identical runs; the broader comparison memory
bound and browser smoke remain open.

The in-memory `compareFinals` count-only mode now checks compared fields without
retaining a `changes` array or a `{identity, before, after}` row for every
matched device. Detail rows are still built for returned results and callbacks,
with the same unchanged callback shape. A 1,205-device regression checks exact
counts, empty result arrays in count-only mode, and the full modified callback
payload. Whole-workbook XLSX parsing, source/result arrays in other paths and
browser smoke remain open under requirement #550.

The count-only path also skips constructing ambiguous identity candidate lists
and wrapper rows when diagnostic arrays and `onAmbiguous` are both disabled.
It still marks every candidate as matched and retains ambiguity counts, so those
records cannot become additions or absences. A regression checks 300 ambiguous
groups and verifies that requesting the callback preserves both candidates.

The streamed Final summary now aggregates field-change and critical-event
counts directly. It creates per-field detail objects only when an
`onModified` callback requests them. A high-cardinality regression compares
1,205 changed devices against full `compareFinals`, checks ordinary and
critical totals and verifies callback values and order. This avoids retaining
per-device change lists in the count-only analytics path.

`createFinalComparisonEventPlan` now classifies matching rows with a scalar
field-difference check instead of allocating full change objects during its
initial pass. Full detail rows are regenerated only when a page or export
requests them. A 1,205-device regression compares plan totals and every
modified detail row with `compareFinals`.

Multi-file field-selection counting now tests whether any selected field is
present without building a filtered `changes` array for every row. The same
scalar check classifies modified rows that become unchanged when their fields
are not selected. A 1,205-device regression checks both selection cases,
unchanged streaming and selected export detail.

Selected-field classification now runs against raw matched before/after rows
and compact event-plan marks. The plan exposes a predicate count pass and
filtered event stream; it builds comparison detail arrays only for selected
page/export rows. A source-level regression prevents the count pass from
calling the detail event generator, and high-cardinality tests verify selected
vendor rows, filtered unchanged rows and totals.

Legacy SQLite row mapping now uses a fixed batch size of 500 instead of 128 to reduce IndexedDB staging/event overhead on large migrations while preserving bounded batches. The focused importer suite and ESLint pass. A complete 1.2-million-row IndexedDB commit has not been performed; browser smoke remains open under the selected code-only verification mode.

Legacy SQLite import cancellation now reaches the paged file-copy stream. An aborted in-flight slice is discarded, later pages are not requested, and failure cleanup deletes the temporary SQLite IndexedDB database. The regression cancels on the first copied page and verifies the cleanup hook.

The streamed archive importer no longer issues a serial IndexedDB `get` before each staged record. The `archiveRecords` compound primary key already enforces the duplicate rule; its `ConstraintError` follows the same rollback and duplicate-error path. Regression `streamed archive staging does not read each record before writing it` stages 500 settings and verifies zero `get()` calls on the archive store plus all records after commit. Duplicate integration regressions verify rejection and cleanup. Full real-database IndexedDB commit and browser smoke remain open.

## Compress duplicate source-row references — 2026-10-22

Single-file analysis now adaptively stores long consecutive source row runs as
inclusive ranges and switches back to a plain number list when sparse ranges
stop saving memory. The result preview displays ranges; CSV emits the original
full row-number list in bounded chunks, and JSON streams the existing
`sourceRows` array shape. Previously saved arrays remain readable. Regressions
cover 1,500 consecutive rows, interleaved and sparse duplicates, exact export,
bounded CSV/JSON output, and a disposable IndexedDB round trip. This removes
range-object growth for interleaved data while retaining compact contiguous
runs; the complete device result array and other paths remain open under
requirement #550.
